# Park: remaining work

This is the single work sequence for finishing Park. **Every numbered stage below is unfinished.** Completed features are summarized here and kept as architecture/evidence at the end; those references are not another stage list. There is no separate delivery numbering. Stage 1 is complete at v22 and its details are in the reference section; the remaining work starts at stage 2. Existing stage numbers stay stable.

The implemented foundation includes the Smithay physical/nested compositor, ordinary native-Wayland application windows, windowed Sandbox, horizontal tiling/free placement, focus/navigation, MRU, overview, width/height controls, pointer manipulation, editable bindings, monitor configuration, rendering/persistence, packaging and session-registration infrastructure. Keep these implementations and extend them.

The latest source contains the inspector creation fix, the shared Record-height settlement correction and the correction to redundant backend-only updates. **Quiet loading/deletion passes at v19:** actual body arrival settles in eight publications and deletion in six, without new input, with unchanged captured identity and a return to idle. **Native permission recovery now passes at v22 in 18.15 seconds**, including actual authenticated refusal, ordinary Refresh/typing/save, retained source/UID/Area/draft/editor and unchanged unrelated Records. Its real refused/recovered captures were inspected. The general live-client correction also passes its source check, three unit cases and the real-network permission/replay/durable-retry case without duplicate body/facts/receipt. Thirteen focused gesture-recognition/viewport model cases and four delivery-buffer cases have scoped passes; later complete desktop gesture integration remains unverified. V19/model artifacts were removed during other work; regenerated v21/v22 evidence and immutable Nix inputs are recorded below. These checkpoints do not establish full-current-source GUI, optimized, installed or physical acceptance.

Work through stages 2–10 in order, testing each feature when it changes; stage 4 closes the remaining combined matrix. Continue independent preparation while a hosted runner/provider/hardware observation is unavailable, retaining its unchecked gate. Remove completed tasks only when their implementation and relevant evidence pass. Missing optional capabilities cannot be fabricated as passes or silently dropped from the approved scope.

Stages 2–7 can be advanced without logging out of Niri, using isolated Cells, sockets, providers and fixtures. Stage 8 needs scheduled real-seat/VT/device observations; stage 9 needs review and scheduled host activation; stage 10 needs the user's actual session selection and work session. This documentation update starts none of those actions.

Login, bars, launchers, lockers, notification services, IME, clipboard history, power services and portal/PipeWire backends remain external choices. Implement only missing general bindings, test first with the user's tools and preserve Sandbox.

The user approves the remaining completion scope and confirms **native Wayland only**. Applications must create native Wayland surfaces without XWayland. Preserve the existing launch boundary that selects Wayland backends and removes inherited `DISPLAY`/`XAUTHORITY`; do not add an X server, XWayland dependency, X11 window-management path or fallback to the parent desktop's X display. Classify applications by their actual connection/surfaces, not by whether their multi-backend binary happens to link an X11 library. An application that only exposes X11 windows is outside Park's support target.

**Workspace refactor** is the new name for the separate work previously called Spacematter. It owns any changes it later requires in Park. This plan finishes Park against its existing canvas, workspace, layout and host contracts: do not import that refactor's tasks, redesign Park for hypothetical future topology, wait for that plan, or alter the approved completion scope because of it. Existing workspace navigation/persistence below is ordinary Park functionality and remains required. Do not edit the Workspace refactor plan as part of Park work.

This is the approved completion scope. A normal workspace containing a native terminal/browser, Lince items and Areas must work through the existing tiled/free and 2D/3D paths without the Workspace refactor or Showoff. Finish the listed features, their usable controls and the delivery/acceptance gates; do not add future topology groundwork, browser embedding or another desktop architecture. Any later Workspace integration changes belong to that refactor even when it needs to modify Park files.

## Completion and architecture contract

Finish the approved features and their usable controls, then close the remaining correctness, performance, package, provider and login gates. A feature is complete only when its ordinary UI/command reaches the shared action, saved state has one owner, failure/cancellation preserves valid state, and the relevant real interaction passes. A registered command, a disabled control or a model-only pass does not finish the corresponding feature.

| Owner | Existing foundation to extend | Boundary to preserve |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Shared desktop model | `crates/interface/src/layout/desktop.rs`, its row/width/transfer/gesture modules and `layout/outputs.rs` | Placement identities, requested geometry, grouping/visibility, stable output assignments and transactions; no second arrangement database |
| Shared policy and native UI | `native/layout/desktop`, shortcuts/configuration, full-Record and Protein-area controls | One action path for toolbar, Operation, configured chord and supported Sandbox interaction; preserve live drafts/focus/scroll |
| Host contracts | `crates/interface/src/host.rs` and `host/{display,input,launch}.rs` | Bounded typed requests/snapshots; no compositor objects or physical-device ownership inside the canvas |
| Park server and input | `crates/park/src/{desktop,server,native_input,session}` | Reuse Smithay's protocol, client/configure, seat/device and backend facilities; Park supplies compositor policy, capability authorization and missing bindings |
| Worker and rendering | `runtime.rs`, `presentation`, `nested/renderer` and the existing physical-output path | One published geometry/input contract, correct GPU lifetimes, output-local mapping and explicit capture policy |
| Persistence | Existing interface configuration transaction, workspace/profile state and Cell Records | Semantic shared settings remain in `interface.lingua`; host device/launch descriptions remain in the existing local Park configuration; owner rules/properties use Records |
| Services and installation | Existing launch descriptions, flake/module/session launcher, selected UWSM and providers | External programs own their services. Add only a demonstrated missing general adapter; no new supervisor, login stack or provider-specific policy |

New settings/actions in this refinement extend those owners. Do not introduce another settings format, command router, inspector, layout mode, frame loop or topology abstraction. Keep current accepted defaults; additional utility actions start unbound and are discoverable through Operation and their existing settings destinations.

Preserve the agreed interaction contract while finishing these features: Mod+T toggles the workspace's tiled/free arrangement; Mod+Shift+T toggles the focused item's floating state. Mod+H/L traverse items within horizontal rows, Mod+J/K traverse rows, and Shift moves through that same structure. Mod+Y/U/I/O selects monitors, Mod+P opens Operation and Mod+Space opens overview. The current registry uses Mod+1–9 to select a workspace and Mod+Shift+1–9 to move the selected item there and follow it; preserve those distinct actions. Mod+Backspace toggles Focus/Unfocus: Unfocus releases keyboard/camera following while retaining the current view and arrangement, and Focus restores an eligible selected/remembered target. Keep Edit leftmost in the bottom-right controls and retain the existing Focus/Unfocus and 2D/3D controls. No Canvas, example-jump, scatter-physics or alternative-layout controls return. These are implemented behavior to preserve, not extra feature checkboxes.

Examples of new types, fields or wire messages below are **implementation designs**, not claims that the current API already contains them. Named current types/paths were checked against the source; preserve the current feature/test checkpoint distinction. Initial numeric bounds below make implementation/test expectations concrete. They are targets to verify, not measurements or permission to lower the existing frame-pacing gates.

Every remaining feature uses this evidence record: source/payload identity; scene/output/scale; actual input/control used; expected state/pixels/Record mutation; observed result; and one of pass, fail or not run with a reason. Keep software correctness, optimized timing, installed-package behavior and physical observations separate. Missing hardware/provider evidence remains explicit without adding an emulation service or an X11 fallback.

| Stage | Remaining result | Work |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [2](#2-finish-workspace-and-interaction-features) | Remaining workspace and interaction features | Implementation and tests |
| [3](#3-finish-desktop-utilities-and-capability-bindings) | Remaining desktop utilities and capability bindings | Implementation and tests |
| [4](#4-close-the-integrated-correctness-matrix) | Final integrated correctness | Integration tests and corrections |
| [5](#5-meet-performance-and-stability-targets) | Measured pacing and stable resources | Profiling and optimized measurements |
| [6](#6-produce-and-verify-the-final-installable-artifact) | Current installable package and frozen fixtures | Packaging and automated acceptance |

“Finish” means the result in each row, not merely reaching its stage number. Stages 2–3 contain the remaining product implementation and focused tests; stage 4 tests their combined behavior. Stages 5–7 close measured performance, delivery and chosen-provider integration, adding code only for demonstrated defects or missing general bindings. Stages 8–10 contain real-seat/host/login acceptance and corrections. No stage requires implementing an external service, XWayland or the separate Workspace refactor. Unsupported hardware must report an honest unavailable capability; supported capabilities still require their listed real-device evidence before being claimed.

## 2. Finish workspace and interaction features

These approved families remain. Rows, width presets, MRU, basic overview, simple pointer manipulation and workspace naming/navigation/reordering already exist. Keep their stable identities, saved content and shared actions.

- [ ] Finish configurable desktop touchpad gestures: three-finger navigation of the row/canvas viewport and workspaces, and four-finger overview. Recognition and viewport model cases have scoped passes; configuration/editor, standard client-protocol binding and physical forwarding still need their full tests. The bounded delivery buffer's four source-module cases now pass against the frozen shared API with warnings denied. Desktop capture, worker delivery/acknowledgements, native viewport/workspace/overview actions and actual-device capability publication are written on the branch, with six shared-controller cases and an ignored real-worker backpressure/device-cancel fixture. This later integration is outside v22 and still needs compilation, execution, ordinary controls/restart and physical acceptance; it is not a complete feature yet. Preserve one/two-finger application input, honor instant transitions and provide keyboard equivalents. Give a recognized desktop gesture one owner for its full lifetime; cancellation/VT changes/lock must not leave an application or drag in a pressed state. Coordinate with the window-management reference's pointer actions instead of adding another move engine.
- [ ] Extend overview with dragging items between rows/workspaces and hover-to-activate during a real file drag. Use bounded, configurable dwell/edge-scroll timing with a visible target; do not switch workspaces just because the pointer crosses a thumbnail. Keep file-drop delivery distinct from moving an application's window, preserve valid client drag serials, and cancel safely on unmap or output change. Basic overview remains a single entry point without new demo buttons or a second layout chooser.
- [ ] Add optional back-and-forth workspace switching, default off, through the settings reference's shared selection path. Named identities, explicit creation, visible numeric mapping, staged creation of missing destinations 1–9, adjacent navigation and workspace reordering are implemented; do not rebuild them. The saved-restart unit path preserves the reordered names, notes and Areas; keep the installed restart workflow in stage 4. Reordering changes numeric positions while retaining every workspace/item identity and authored content. Unlike Niri's empty-workspace cleanup, absence of application windows never makes a Lince workspace disposable; never delete or overwrite a workspace to obtain a number. Saved Sands, Areas, models and names survive all normal navigation.
- [ ] Finish per-output workspace/view/focus affinity using the existing `Assignments` relocation/reconnect path and the window-management reference's two-output recovery fixture. Complete the saved view/focus and deliberate-user-move cases, including safe return while another output remains actively in use: restoration must not steal its keyboard focus or duplicate a workspace. Treat a deliberate user move as the new affinity. Serialize this through the existing workspace/output state, not a parallel desktop database. Extend the existing physical hotplug acceptance rather than claiming it from the virtual-output fixture.
- [ ] Expose click-to-focus, optional pointer-follow focus and optional cursor warping as distinct settings, defaulting to click focus with no warp. Preserve the window-management reference's implemented active-application-family ordering and passing coplanar Qt/native tests; do not rebuild that selection policy or move authored spatial depth to raise a window. Extend actual pixel/input verification through MRU, authorized activation and the optional policies. A pointer crossing a moving component must not repeatedly retarget a locked camera. Respect explicit canvas Unfocus, activation authorization, popup ownership and urgent-window notifications. An urgent app may indicate attention; it must not bypass focus/lock policy to jump the camera.
- [ ] Add per-application opening rules for initial workspace/output, size, floating/maximize and focus behavior through the existing window Record/assertion/Area mechanisms and the implemented Window properties. Distinguish one-shot initial placement from continuously applied Area behavior so focus changes do not repeatedly reinsert a window. Application titles/app IDs are matching metadata, not authority to launch programs or acquire privileged protocol capabilities. Avoid a second competing rules engine.
- [ ] Add bounded live reload for external edits of interface.lingua. Watch the directory as well as the selected file so atomic replacement/symlink updates work. Parse and validate the entire proposed configuration, then publish the same transaction used by the editor; invalid input retains the last good active configuration and shows a useful error. Do not overwrite an open draft silently: detect an external revision change and offer reload/rebase/cancel. A write by Park must not produce an infinite save/reload loop or run configuration scripts.
- [ ] Expose command invocation and relevant window/workspace/output change events to external automation through the existing authorized management/host boundaries, with typed command IDs and explicit errors. Add only missing general bindings. Avoid an unrestricted command socket or importing another compositor's API. Use the same actions as the UI, rate/bound event queues, handle disappearing targets by identity and reject privileged actions without the correct scope. Reuse existing launch descriptions for external commands.

### Interaction architecture and implementation order

Implement the checklist in its listed order. Extend the existing shared model/actions and native controls first; bind physical events and authorized requests through Park afterward. Keep a captured root/workspace/output and identity for an interaction's lifetime. Changing focus, settings or pointer location cannot retarget an in-flight gesture, drag or automation request.

The current `layout::desktop::Gesture` owns pointer move/resize; it does not implement the requested multi-finger desktop gestures. The separate `host/input/gestures.rs` recognizer has captured settings, axis locking, absolute pan progress and one terminal decision. The physical adapter forwards client-owned swipe/pinch/hold events through device-owned sequences and Smithay's pointer-gesture facility, while keeping device-setting deferral. The later branch integration now selects eligible desktop swipe ownership, delivers recognized values over the existing worker channel and reaches the shared controller in `native/layout/desktop/gestures/viewport.rs`. That route is written, not yet compiled or accepted. Keep recognition/desktop actions in the shared policy so a Sandbox host can supply the same typed stream if available. Today's pointer settings already handle tap, natural scroll, handedness and acceleration; focus-follow and cursor-warp are additional policies, not reasons to replace that editor.

### Gesture and overview bounds

Use normalized logical deltas from the existing input mapping. Initial tunable targets are sensitivity 1.0, dominant-axis lock after 12 logical units with a 1.5× dominance ratio, and an 80-unit workspace/overview commit threshold. Bound sensitivity to 0.1–8 and thresholds to 16–400 logical units. A cancelled gesture restores the saved view/selection; a large completed gesture still changes at most one workspace. Keep instant transitions and the existing spatial-anchor update, with no second scroll accumulator.

Enable these mappings initially only when the current Park backend exposes the required gesture stream, with one visible enable/disable setting. Capture ownership at Begin by the enabled finger-count mapping, before axis recognition; a reserved sequence that never reaches a configured axis/threshold cancels as a desktop no-op, never a late client Begin. Disabled/unmapped finger counts remain client-owned for their complete sequence. Positive vertical three-finger travel selects the next workspace in the existing order, negative selects the previous; four-finger downward opens overview and upward closes it, with unavailable/redundant transitions as no-ops. Viewport pan uses the existing canvas direction/normalization once. Expose this mapping in settings rather than inferring it from animation.

Recognize the original ordered deltas before coalescing transport updates. Axis lock depends on the travelled path; combining a diagonal prefix into its final delta before recognition can choose a different action. The written recognizer already returns absolute `horizontal_travel` and a `GestureResult`; reuse these values instead of implementing a second recognizer in the worker.

Extend the existing worker input enum with the delivery types already written in `host/input/gesture_delivery.rs`; do not create competing transport types. Their current shape is below. `owner` is the existing opaque runtime owner token from the published workspace snapshot. It is valid only for that worker lifetime, is revalidated before use, and is neither durable identity nor authority for an external caller. Reuse the current host contract rather than inventing a replacement root-ID system for the Workspace refactor.

```rust
struct GestureContext {
    sequence: u64,
    owner: u64,
    workspace: u64,
    output: OutputId,
    fingers: u32,
}

enum GestureDelivery {
    Begin(GestureContext),
    Pan(GestureProgress),
    Finish(GestureCompletion),
}
```

Reject non-finite deltas and malformed/reused lifetimes. Capture settings, device and eligible root/workspace/output at Begin; later focus or pointer changes cannot retarget the sequence. Respect current modal/grab/shortcut-inhibition/lock ownership when deciding whether desktop capture is eligible. Removal, lock, VT departure or output loss terminates the captured sequence once through the same cancellation path. Settings changes apply to the next Begin. An unclaimed lifetime goes to Smithay's client path; a desktop-owned no-op/cancel never becomes a late application Begin.

The existing worker input channel holds 128 messages. `GestureDeliveryBuffer` is written to keep at most one unacknowledged delivery plus one bounded pending absolute progress/terminal state. Connect `next_delivery()` to `try_send`; call `sent(input_serial)` only after acceptance and `acknowledge(completed.input_serial)` on the existing published completion path. A full channel retains the same delivery for the next opportunity. Replace pending pan progress only within the same sequence; retain Begin/Finish ordering and retry a terminal delivery after backpressure rather than dropping it. Cancellation before an unsent Begin discards that sequence; cancellation after Begin replaces a queued commit with Cancel and does not duplicate an already sent terminal. Do not block the compositor thread or introduce another event loop. If the previous terminal has not been accepted, a new reserved desktop sequence must be rejected/cancelled with bounded feedback; it cannot overwrite the previous owner or hand a partial sequence to a client. Run the four written buffer cases and verify these bounds through the actual worker with 10,000 synthetic updates, including End/Cancel and device loss.

At worker Begin, capture the existing valid view/focus return state and tiled model scroll. Use the written `Workspace::scroll_to` operation and the model's existing solve/usable viewport bounds. Its three regressions preserve sizes/focus/authored homes, fixed floating geometry, current usable bounds, atomic rejection and saved tiled scroll through free mode. Free 2D pan uses `CanvasView`, and spatial pan uses the existing output projection/camera path without adding a topology abstraction. Apply absolute travel against the captured start so coalescing/retries cannot apply a delta twice. A horizontal swipe moves the view as updates arrive; an End-only jump does not pass. Do not move authored items or reconfigure clients to pan.

Cancellation restores valid saved view/selection and tiled scroll exactly. The existing focus return-state helper does not save the model scroll, and resuming focus can reveal a target; restore the captured scroll after that operation so cancellation cannot leave a different viewport. Revalidate context before every commit. A new ordinary action, modal takeover or changed output/workspace invalidates the old preview before processing the new interaction; a delayed gesture End cannot switch that newer workspace or restore an obsolete view over it. Output geometry changes cancel affected gestures; unrelated client frame updates do not.

Wire `GestureSupport` from the actual usable backend stream only after the full route exists, and publish saved settings through the current frame/input contract. A saved enable preference alone is not a capability. The existing Park-tab page must show unavailable support honestly, preserve its draft and retain keyboard equivalents in Sandbox/nested hosts without a gesture stream. Sandbox must not open global libinput to compensate.

For example, four 25-unit vertical updates followed by End select one adjacent workspace; two identical End messages still select only once. A 40-unit pan followed by Cancel restores the initial state. Before/after this test, a client receiving an unclaimed sequence must get one Begin, ordered travel and one End/Cancel. Overflow cannot discard terminal cancellation. Save a non-default 175% sensitivity/112-unit threshold, restart, and verify those values and a pre-existing rebound command survive together. Run the written ten recognizer, three native editor, one configuration-codec and three standard-protocol cases, then add the missing real worker pan/cancel/context/backpressure and normal-input recovery fixtures. These test counts describe the current written set, not passing results or a limit on necessary regressions.

For file-drag hover, start with 400 ms dwell, configurable within 100–2000 ms. Leaving/re-entering a target resets dwell; pointer travel across thumbnails alone does not switch workspaces. Use a bounded edge-scroll velocity, initially at most one usable viewport per second, and elapsed-time integration rather than a frame-count step. Show one destination outline. Unmap, output removal, lock and owner changes cancel safely with zero stuck buttons/gestures and no unintended drop.

### Rule precedence and reload transactions

Use sparse initial properties with this precedence: explicit owner values for that window, then the first matching ordered application rule, then its retained normal/profile intent, then generic opening defaults. Unspecified fields inherit from the next source. Existing client constraints still resolve effective pixels. Keep a stable rule order with Record UID as a tie-breaker, using the existing bounded condition machinery rather than introducing script evaluation or another rule engine.

Capture available app metadata at initial management; rules use a startup-loaded owner-rule snapshot and do not wait for asynchronous application-Record creation. A late app-ID/title change cannot trigger automatic repositioning. An explicit Reapply action may evaluate the selected existing item through the same transaction. Focus preferences never bypass activation/lock authorization, and no rule can launch a program or add protocol capabilities. Continuous Area influence stays on its existing path rather than repeatedly executing an opening rule.

Example conflict: a local draft changes the width chord while an external edit changes Operation's chord. A reviewed rebase can keep both independent edits. If both change the width chord, retain both candidate values in the existing conflict UI and require resolution before Save. Preserve effective modifier ownership and active input sequences while publishing settings.

Reuse the existing 256 KiB configuration limit, add a 150 ms event debounce with a 500 ms maximum postponement from the first event in a burst, and ignore self-written revisions after the existing successful save. Parse/validate a complete replacement before changing active values; no partially applied registry, indefinite debounce starvation or unbounded frame-by-frame file polling. Saving after an external revision still uses the same stale-origin check and private atomic write. Restart must load the accepted configuration through the existing startup path.

### Missing automation binding

The existing `server/management.rs` implements standard foreign-toplevel operations, not arbitrary typed Park commands. Use a small optional Park management Wayland extension for the missing command/event surface; do not reinterpret a standard protocol's fields or add another unrestricted socket/daemon. A proposed `DesktopControl` launch capability gates desktop commands; retain `WindowList` for eligible read-only window data and each existing specific capability for window/output/capture operations. Update the capability validator/editor limits together if adding this enum value; the current source has six capabilities and a six-entry validation bound.

Wire messages carry a request ID, registered command ID, typed bounded arguments and explicit target identity/context. They contain no shell expression, Bevy entity, mutable “current window” lookup or client-supplied permission grant. Resolve live generation/lifetime at execution and return one correlated result. Use an existing action's availability and lock policy; ordinary application credentials/title/app ID never create authority.

Initial bounds: at most 16 in-flight requests and 16 KiB of arguments per request, 64 requests/second with a 32-request burst, and 256 queued events per observer. Coalesce replaceable state events; overflow publishes a resync requirement and a fresh bounded snapshot rather than silently losing a terminal result or accumulating history. Test floods, disconnects, reused surface identities, lock and unauthorized callers. This optional extension does not change the standard contracts used by other bars/launchers.

Exit: each feature has its normal settings/Operation/UI destination, persistence and focused input/lifetime tests. Gestures reuse Smithay and the worker/model; opening rules reuse Record/assertion/Area mechanisms. Shared behavior remains available in Sandbox where its host permits it.

## 3. Finish desktop utilities and capability bindings

These accepted features are mostly not implemented. Add missing Lince policy/UI/general bindings, reusing Smithay and external services. Keep the single tiled/free arrangement and existing controls. Capability-gate supported hardware; provider/device acceptance continues in stages 7–8.

- [ ] Complete external screenshot and monitor/window sharing integration with a visible active-sharing indicator and per-window capture-exclusion setting. Reuse the selected portal/PipeWire/capture provider and existing authorization. Enforce exclusion in every supported capture path, not just in thumbnail UI; output composition must mask excluded content where required. Define capture of popups/transients and interaction with lock/output changes, and clear indicators when the provider disconnects. Lince does not become a replacement portal or recording service.
- [ ] Add **windowed fullscreen** as a distinct application action: the client receives fullscreen state while its compositor-controlled rectangle remains bounded. Expose its active state and restore the previous state/geometry on exit. Keep normal fullscreen, maximize and tiling width independent, and handle a client declining/changing its configure without looping. This supports presentation workflows without a browser-specific implementation.
- [ ] Add explicit compact/tabbed groups inside a normal row slot: several native/application items share the slot, one is visible, and selection/close/reorder remain deterministic. It is grouping within the single tiled arrangement, not a workspace Columns/Tabbed layout switch. Respect per-item constraints, modal children, focus, capture and lifetime; a hidden member must not continue receiving ordinary input. Keep original identities/content and restore individual placement on ungroup. No new permanent toolbar button is required.
- [ ] Add user-selected scratch windows as a general hide/show utility for an existing terminal/calculator/other item. Preserve a home workspace/rectangle and explicit ownership while shown on another workspace; do not duplicate the live surface, relaunch it unnecessarily or auto-assign all floating windows to scratch. On target close remove the stale entry; lock blocks reveal. A chosen external launcher/supervisor still owns launching/services. This utility was explicitly present in the accepted useful-family list; no PaperWM-specific minimap, scratch-layer key scheme or shell dependency is adopted.
- [ ] Finish keyboard/screen-reader accessibility for Park's shell controls, overview, switcher, configuration errors and confirmation flows using existing Bevy/AccessKit and the host accessibility bridge. Test the chosen external reader in a real Park session without registering a competing global bridge in nested Niri. Add output-level desktop magnification distinct from canvas/world zoom; transform input/overlay/cursor mapping coherently and preserve camera-follow invariants. Keep text labels and keyboard recovery usable at high scale.
- [ ] Extend output settings with rotation, VRR and supported color/HDR controls using Smithay/backend/driver capabilities and the existing test/apply/rollback path. Do not assume advertised DRM properties mean the full color pipeline is correct. Preserve per-output color/format state through composition, surface import, capture and hotplug; validate with actual supported hardware. Capability-gate controls and report unavailable features, leaving unsupported/unverified requested work explicit. Nightlight/color-provider integration remains external rather than a hardcoded service.
- [ ] Bind laptop lid, idle, output power-off, suspend and wake policy to the user's selected external power/idle services. Park supplies only missing device/output/session adapters and safe recovery. Do not implement a battery daemon, locker, login manager or supervisor. Retain a usable output when the lid closes with an external monitor and verify input/scanout restoration on wake through the physical acceptance path.
- [ ] Extend supported touch/tablet/peripheral mapping through the existing input backend, with output selection/calibration and usable pointer/button/gesture routing. Expose only supported device settings and preserve application ownership/grabs. Keep software fixtures for event invariants separate from actual-device acceptance; do not claim hardware support based on the absence of crashes.
- [ ] Reuse existing Lince theme/shader controls for optional compositor borders, shadows, blur and transitions; add only missing general rendering bindings and budgets. Defaults preserve instant transitions and accessible focus feedback. Effect toggles cannot alter layout, revive removed demo chrome or introduce relative camera-follow lag. Measure extra damage/GPU/memory cost in release mode, respect reduced-motion choices and keep lock/capture rendering correct. This is the accepted visual-effects family, not approval for Wayfire-style effect/plugin architecture.

### Utility implementation contracts

| Family | Architecture and ordinary UI | Concrete acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Capture privacy and sharing | Extend existing `server/capture.rs`, output/window capture rendering and the host snapshot with effective exclusion and active sessions. Persist owner policy through the existing window Record/native-item settings, with a normal Window properties control and existing status/notification surface. | A private window remains visible to its owner but never appears in an authorized external output/window capture, including transient menus, previews and effects. Active-session state clears on stop/disconnect/lock; an unavailable portal is explained rather than replaced. |
| Windowed fullscreen | Add a distinct item command/state using the existing application configure/restore transaction; expose it in Operation/item controls, initially unbound. Keep client fullscreen intent separate from normal compositor fullscreen/maximize. | The client receives fullscreen state while its rectangle remains bounded by the same tiled/free slot and bar reservations. The surrounding canvas/projection remains usable. Exiting restores the exact prior requested geometry/state; rejected/stale client configures cannot loop. |
| Compact groups | Add one group/slot relation to the existing workspace desktop model, retaining each member's placement identity and original home. Render an inline scrollable member selector through normal item controls; no workspace layout switch. | Group A/B/C occupies one row slot. Selecting B exposes only B and its eligible owned children; hidden members receive no normal pointer/keyboard/IME/drop target. Closing/reordering/ungrouping preserves deterministic focus and original identities/content. |
| Scratch windows | Add an explicit scratch association/home to existing item state and reuse workspace/output transfer and visibility policy. Expose mark/show/hide/unmark through Operation/ordinary item controls, initially unbound. | Mark an existing terminal, hide it, reveal that same live surface on another workspace, hide it and return to the previous valid focus. No clone or implicit launch. Close removes the live scratch entry; lock blocks reveal. |
| Accessibility and magnification | Extend existing native keyboard semantics/Bevy AccessKit and the host bridge; add an output magnification transform to the existing output/input mapping, separate from world-camera zoom. Use Park's existing settings/Operation destinations. | All shell controls and confirmation/error paths are keyboard reachable and named; the chosen reader reports the selected control/value/error. Magnification transforms the visible output and inverse input/caret mapping once, with usable reset and no camera-follow lag. |
| Advanced outputs | Extend `host/display.rs`, the existing display test/preview/keep/revert transaction, backend capability discovery and composition/import/capture metadata. Add only supported rotation/VRR/color controls to the current monitor editor. | Test and revert rotation without swapped hit axes, configure loops or stale popups; VRR/color requests are capability-validated. HDR is offered only with an implemented end-to-end format/transfer/color path, not from a DRM flag alone. |
| Power/lid/idle | Bind the chosen external power/idle/locker manager through existing session/output capabilities and backend events. Park only supplies missing device/output state handoffs and recovery. | With an external output active, the selected lid policy preserves a usable output. Output-off retains clients/Cell; wake restores real output/input mapping and correct protection state. No battery daemon or independent idle timer service is added. |
| Touch/tablet/peripherals | Extend existing `session/devices.rs`, input snapshots and native settings with supported mapping/calibration/button controls. Keep one device registry and capture owner. | A device mapped to output B cannot inject into A; rotation/scale/calibration agree with physical pixels. Removal during a sequence sends one cancellation and cannot leave pressed input. Unsupported settings remain unchanged with a useful reason. |
| Optional effects | Reuse existing Lince shader/theme controls, renderer budgeting/damage and item visibility. Put compositor effect toggles in existing appearance settings; do not add a plugin/effect engine. | Borders/shadows/blur/fades preserve layout, input, privacy/lock and resource lifetime. Defaults remain instant/off for added motion/effects, reduced-motion is respected, and camera/target motion is never delayed for appearance. |

### Capture must exclude before composition can leak content

Current capture supports authorized output/window jobs and bounded SHM buffers; it does not establish a zero-copy PipeWire or HDR capture path. Keep the existing provider/protocol adapters and current 16-megapixel/job constraints unless a measured supported case requires a reviewed limit change. Do not introduce a second screenshot/recording service or promise DMA-BUF capture from a passing SHM test.

Resolve privacy from captured placement/Record identity, never app title. A private parent makes its subsurfaces, owned popups/transients and derived previews private. For direct window capture, refuse/stop the private source through the existing failure path. For output capture, omit or redact private contributions before blur, shadows, preview rendering or shader composition can sample them. Local owner presentation continues normally. A final black rectangle pasted over already sampled private pixels is insufficient.

Carry effective privacy/provenance into both native previews and external composition. If an existing flattened native texture cannot yet distinguish public/private contributions safely, fail closed for that affected capture region/frame until the binding is correct; do not call a potentially leaking image a successful capture. Keep lock/output-change checks at job queue and completion boundaries so an older queued job cannot publish an unsafe frame after the policy changes.

The following is a proposed policy value, not an existing API or a second ownership store:

```rust
enum CaptureVisibility {
    Public,
    Excluded,
}

struct EffectiveCapturePolicy {
    item: ItemId,
    visibility: CaptureVisibility,
    revision: u64,
}
```

Test actual output and window capture pixels in flat/spatial, tiled/free scenes; include a private parent with a popup outside its rectangle, an overview preview, active blur, group switching, scratch hide/reveal, resize, output removal and lock. Use distinctive private patterns plus a public control window: private contributions must be absent while expected public content remains visible and updates normally. An entirely black unprotected capture cannot pass merely because it hides the private pattern. The pass criterion is **zero private pixels**, not a hidden thumbnail or a status flag. Existing capture authorization remains required even for public content.

Show the actual authorized capture session/provider and target, including paused/live state only when known from the protocol/provider. A live capture connection is not proof that an external program is recording to disk. Stop/disconnect/lock must clear state without leaving an indicator or busy render loop. An optional provider Stop action uses its declared general binding; client-reported identity cannot grant that action extra authority.

### Window utilities share placement and visibility

Windowed fullscreen must not enter the existing normal-fullscreen flat-output normalization or change a spatial camera. Preserve previous width/height/float/maximize intent in the same item's existing restore state, configure the client with the bounded logical size, and accept only current configure/buffer generations. Keep normal fullscreen requests on their existing route unless the owner explicitly chose the new action. Native items without an application protocol role expose the action as unavailable rather than pretending to configure a client.

A compact group's slot has requested width/height; its active member resolves its own live constraints within that slot, using the existing oversized-client clipping policy where needed. Switching members cannot resize neighbours or rewrite hidden members' authored homes. Hidden members keep their live client identity and normal throttling but no ordinary input endpoint; do not suspend arbitrary application processes. Owned modal children follow the active member's visibility/eligibility.

Example: group A and B in a half-width slot, switch A→B twenty times, reorder members, close B and ungroup A. There must still be one A surface/Record/placement, no B input after close, no duplicate row member, no neighbour size change and no transient-preview geometry persisted. Restore an ungrouped native item's content and an application's retained home through their existing save owners.

Scratch uses those same visibility/transfer primitives. Repeated show/hide is one state change per distinct action; a command while locked cannot queue a reveal for later. Native content stays with its existing persistent item, and closed application scratch entries do not turn into zombie placements or automatic relaunches at cold startup. Do not infer scratch membership from “floating”.

### Output, accessibility and effect bounds

For rotation/magnification, publish one authoritative final output transform and its inverse for client hit testing, native content, popups, cursor, drag, caret/IME and capture. Compose the display transform after the existing canvas/world projection; this is an ordinary compositor mapping change, not groundwork for the separate Workspace refactor. Preserve usable-area/bar reservations and the original display-preview rollback token/deadline.

Initial magnification range is 1×–4×, default 1×. Test at 1×/2×/4× with pointer and keyboard/caret targets, bars and lock. Keep reset reachable through a named keyboard-accessible action and the settings control. At 360 physical pixels/150% and 1000 pixels/125%, the Park controls, group selector and required confirmation buttons remain reachable by keyboard and normal scrolling; do not solve narrow UI by moving Edit or restoring removed demo controls.

Use supported transform cases for 0°/90°/180°/270° and verify inverse hit coordinates against rendered content, not against a second copy of the same faulty transform. Extend typed output capabilities/settings together; today's `OutputSettings` contains mode/position/scale only. Validate all candidate heads before publishing, keep the existing 15-second confirmation/revert behavior, and restore prior mode/transform/color/resource state on failed test, timeout, cancellation or hotplug.

Capability discovery, software negotiation/rollback and unavailable-state UI can be tested without an HDR/VRR display. Actual supported-hardware evidence remains separate and is required before claiming those output capabilities work. Preserve color/alpha/format correctness through import, composition and capture; an unimplemented color pipeline is not “supported but hidden”. External nightlight/power/reader services remain external.

The current native accessibility plugin supplies roles, labels, protected-input values and live status; it does not establish a working platform bridge for Park's offscreen shell. Sandbox enables Bevy's Winit/Unix accessibility integration, while Park's presentation worker needs the missing host binding. Reuse AccessKit's platform adapter for tree publication/action requests, keeping stable node identity and the existing native actions; do not implement a screen reader, a second accessibility tree or a new AT-SPI service. Publish only eligible shell nodes with their final output bounds, retire removed nodes and never expose hidden/protected text as ordinary values. Test the bridge on a private accessibility/D-Bus fixture before the chosen reader's physical session acceptance. A labelled button or passing metadata unit test alone does not close this task.

For new optional effects, begin with an extra-cost target of ≤1 ms median and ≤2 ms p95 per 1366×768 output on the measured baseline GPU, with the total stage-5 pacing gates still met. Record settings/output/GPU when measuring; this is a proposed budget to verify. Under pressure reduce/disable optional effect work through the existing budgets before reducing an actively edited surface or changing client sizes. Twenty enable/disable and close/reopen cycles must return tracked images/cameras to their normal post-warmup baseline, without retained private pixels or unfinished GPU use.

Exit: each family has its approved controls, ownership/lifetime behavior and software tests. A visible control alone proves neither hardware nor provider support; retain unperformed acceptance explicitly.

### Combined correctness matrix and pass metrics

Use the existing xtask preparation/validation stages and private compositor/Cell fixtures. Cover the four basic scenes—flat free, flat tiled, spatial free and spatial tiled—with wide/narrow and scaled-output representatives. Preserve the existing 1000-physical-pixel/125% and 360-pixel/150% interaction cases, plus focused 100%/200%, nonzero-origin, companion-output and bar cases. Select combinations that exercise each boundary; do not multiply every unrelated fixture into an unnecessary full Cartesian product.

| Boundary | Required real interaction | Pass metric |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Commands and ownership | Actual chord press/release, held repeat, two distinct presses batched in one update, rebound chord, capture/Operation/modal handoff, app/native typing afterward | One action per distinct non-repeating press, no leaked swallowed event, no stuck modifier/button and no wrong input owner |
| Widths and rows | Mod+R through full/half/third/two-thirds, neighbour/height retention, shared-row limits, floating/retile, fullscreen/maximize and output/bar changes | Exact requested ratios remain independent of constrained pixels. With 900 usable logical units and minimum 450: full/half/third/two-thirds/full resolves to 900/450/450/600/900 without trapping the cycle |
| MRU and lifetime | Forward/reverse preview, hold-modifier release, Escape, target/dialog close, workspace/output round trip | One accepted commit or cancellation, unchanged draft/view on cancel, no reused-identity target and no background-map focus theft |
| Focus/camera | Follow a moving native item and an actual client, horizontal/inter-row tiled reveal, first 2D→3D entry and output handoff | Same simulation state drives target and camera in a published frame; zero-frame relative follow delay, ≤1 physical pixel of rounding drift and no accumulating drift in 120 unchanged/following frames |
| Outputs and mapping | Test/preview/keep/revert/timeout, scale/rotation, popup outside parent bounds, native editor/caret, pointer drag and companion window | Rendered hit/caret target agrees within one physical pixel of raster rounding; only intentional geometry changes configure clients; failed transactions leave no partial output state |
| Record/property mutations | Ordinary and recovery edits to captured UID with unrelated control Record and window closure | Exact intended body/assertion change, zero unrelated writes, retained inspector/draft/workspace and read-only metadata; all nine Qt cases pass |
| New feature visibility/authority | Group/scratch hide/reveal, privacy capture, gesture/drag cancellation, config reload and automation flood/stale requests | Hidden/locked/unavailable targets receive zero ordinary input or unauthorized mutation; no duplicate placement, leaked private pixel or unbounded queue |
| Persistence and restart | Save modified configuration/workspace/profile through ordinary controls, dispose the real owner and reopen the same directory | Same authored identities/content/requested sizes, no saved transient preview, no zombie client or automatic program launch; Sandbox and Park use their existing startup configuration path |

The one-pixel allowances cover final raster rounding, not a smoothing delay, incorrect coordinate origin or missing camera update. Compare independently expected coordinates, actual composed pixels and intended input recipient; comparing two copies of the same transform is insufficient. Client buffer cadence and worker rendering rates may differ, but a published locked-follow frame cannot use the target's current position with an older camera position.

Start asynchronous diagnostic deadlines after the fixture is ready, not during compilation/font/GPU startup. Timeouts fail the affected case; increasing a deadline is not a performance correction. Product interactions use the normal keyboard/pointer/control path. Passive observation may locate a widget or inspect published state, but cannot set its focus/text, invoke its action, fabricate a successful Cell reply or force a frame in the quiet-wakeup case.

### Checkpoint and execution discipline

Implement one coherent change, freeze a source manifest and compile the relevant check/test executables once; reuse their frozen binaries for focused correctness/capture diagnosis. Run the appropriate source checks after source-affecting changes and repeat broader checks when affected contracts change. Do not rebuild the entire GUI/package for every pointer observation or repeat unchanged passing suites while a known failure remains undiagnosed.

When concurrent branch edits invalidate that input, compile from a verified validation export rather than repeatedly compiling a moving tree. The v18/v19 target-local inputs and archives were removed during ongoing validation, so they cannot serve as available current evidence. V20 captures 3,169 selected files containing code/configuration, fixtures, public assets, licenses, packaging and the pinned Nix environment. Source/export SHA-256 checks match. Its immutable input is `/nix/store/sflys79sqhknaxbacgalx88pnl777smg-lince-park-validation-source`; the selected list/manifests are generated under `target/park-validation/recovery-01a13134-v20-*`. The export excludes the old web crate, live Cell, owner configuration and Workspace plan. Original implementation files and Records stay on the current branch; this is a validation artifact, not an alternate implementation checkout.

Keep each immutable input fixed through its compilation and runtime fixtures. Build outputs remain under `target`, with four jobs and warnings denied. After the target-local artifacts disappeared, v20 seeds the normal target's dependency/build/fingerprint directories from the available warm check cache and uses the project's normal `target-cpu=native` flags; it does not copy a Cargo lock or create a cold second dependency cache. Cached files are an optimization, not acceptance evidence. Record these flags separately from the earlier explicit mold-linker checkpoint. Reuse exact completed executables for diagnosis, preserve their hashes, and do not restart a live job merely because an observation times out. New source corrections require a new immutable input after affected compiler/fixture handles are terminal. Final stages 4/6 still need the final reviewed input, complete embedded-asset audit and actual installed payload.

V20's transport check reaches the new network fixture and finds its incorrect `Wire::serve().await.unwrap()` call; `serve` returns unit. The fixture is corrected. V21's immutable source is `/nix/store/c50n2c437dv9a0cq8dmcka2a0snjgwnz-lince-park-validation-source`. Compile a verified real copy at the stable `target/park-validation/park-source` path using `--manifest-path`; retain the immutable input as its recovery source. Copying changed inputs must give them current modification times. Do not switch a source symlink between Nix inputs whose files all have normalized timestamps and assume Cargo will notice changed content. The v21 byte manifest verifies the compile copy; later input updates wait for its compiler/fixtures to finish and preserve unchanged compiler/package paths for incremental reuse. Actual unit/network/native runs below determine acceptance.

V21's `transport --features mcp,terminal --lib --tests` source check passes with warnings denied in 3m31s; its three unit cases and initial real-network case pass. V22 changes only that network test to add native durable autosave/receipt coverage: its check passes in 3.54 seconds and the exact case passes in 4.53 seconds. The matching immutable source is `/nix/store/bw9yjhq4rgrfgh6r9nh8dvcrhhpfyrgi-lince-park-validation-source`; the stable compile directory is byte-verified against `recovery-01a13134-v22-source.sha256`. The Park session/server-test library check passes with warnings denied in 8m13s and test compilation in 15m34s. Its frozen executable passes visible native permission recovery in 18.15 seconds and quiet loading/deletion in 17.04 seconds, with actual captures inspected. Native/Record unit-test compilation and the nine-case Qt selection are currently running against that same fixed source.

The stale synchronous-image-loader test calls found at v18 are corrected on the branch. V19 builds the Park test executable and runs the recovery cases described above; its broader invocation exits 101 while the validation directory disappears and establishes no full interface-test pass. Regenerate the relevant source/test targets from v20 rather than treating that missing diagnostic/artifact as an unrelated feature failure or bypassing the interface gate. Narrow dependency/test corrections do not add Workspace-refactor features to Park's scope.

For example, the current Park source gate is an existing command, not a new runner:

```sh
nix develop --option eval-cache false .#interface --command cargo check --locked -p lince-park --all-features --all-targets
```

Use the established task-local caches/scratch and Cargo target under `target`, with stable flags and warnings denied. Record stopped compilation as stopped, an empty test selection as unrun, and ignored GPU cases separately from executed cases. Reuse debug correctness executables for diagnosis; only release binaries establish optimized pacing, and only the accepted installed payload establishes packaging/login behavior.

Failures caused solely by the separate Workspace refactor belong to that refactor's integration work. Report the exact changed contract/checkpoint and continue independent Park tasks; do not silently convert that refactor into new Park scope or invent another architecture. Park's own feature regressions remain Park's responsibility.

Exit: relevant combined input/editor/IME, rows/focus/MRU/resize, output/scale, feature-lifetime and persistence checks pass for their recorded checkpoint. Provider and hardware evidence remain stages 7–8; final installed restart/login remains stages 6 and 10.

## 5. Meet performance and stability targets

Correctness passes do not close existing pacing failures. Use an optimized candidate and quiet machine, with no compilation during timing. Reuse tracing/analyzer/soak tools and preserve buffer lifetime/synchronization while correcting measured costs.

- [ ] Profile and address the remaining frame-time cost before claiming consistently smooth playback at the output refresh rate. Preserve the failing 44.5 FPS stress sample and distinguish browser-produced video frames, fresh compositor buffers and actual physical presentation. Cover ordinary workspaces and Showoff, windowed/fullscreen playback, popup transitions, and multiple outputs with and without a bar. Do not count repaint callbacks as presentation or lower the acceptance target to hide a miss.
- [ ] Repeat quiet optimized video/Showoff/idle/lifecycle and resource checks for the final implementation: windowed/fullscreen, one/two outputs, with/without a bar, flat/spatial WGSL/GLB/map/physics and popup transitions. Use 10-second warmup, 60-second video samples and three quiet runs. For 60 Hz ordinary work retain the proposed ≥57 fresh FPS, p95 ≤25 ms and no repeated interaction stalls >100 ms; retain Showoff's 45 FPS stress floor and every historical miss. Distinguish browser content, fresh compositor buffers and physical presentation; nested scanout stays null. Check bounded resources after warmup in idle and active soaks. The ≥90-minute interactive session is stage 10.

### Performance acceptance and attribution

| Workload | Measurement | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Ordinary native-Wayland video/workflow at 60 Hz | Three quiet runs, each 10-second warmup + 60 seconds measured; windowed/fullscreen, one/two outputs, bar on/off representatives | ≥57 fresh compositor FPS and p95 fresh-frame interval ≤25 ms. Record every >100 ms interaction stall and eliminate repeated compositor-caused stalls. Browser-produced frames/drops remain a separate series |
| Showoff stress | Same durations, flat/spatial WGSL/GLB/map/physics, normal client/video and popup transitions | ≥45 fresh FPS for the documented stress floor. Preserve all historical misses and report p95/max; this floor does not imply refresh-rate playback |
| True idle | No animation/video/physics/blinking editor; warm caches, then 60 seconds of worker/process observation | No self-sustaining frame publication. Backend notifications still wake correctly. Report CPU/wake counts rather than hiding a continuous loop behind an average FPS |
| Lifecycle stability | Twenty create/map/resize/fullscreen/restore/close cycles, followed by settled idle; separate ≥30-minute idle and active soaks | No monotonic descriptor/image/buffer growth across the last ten comparable cycles; bounded threads/resources after warmup. Closed producers release their resources after valid GPU retirement; legitimate stable caches are identified |
| Daily use | At least 90 minutes using the installed session in stage 10 | No crash, lost saved content, wrong-target input/mutation or protection failure; resource/frame-time limits remain documented against the chosen workload/hardware |

Use already available native-update, client-import, export/copy/fence, composition and presentation trace stages. Change the measured bottleneck and repeat the affected quiet workload with the same payload/settings; do not replace Smithay, add another renderer or disable synchronization without evidence requiring that change. CPU query work and GPU copy/fence wait are different costs even if they appear in the same frame.

Every timing report records source/payload hashes, optimization/features, output modes/scales, provider/client versions, trace enabled/disabled, warmup/duration and competing-work status. Trace overhead is diagnostic; final timing runs use the agreed normal configuration. CPU/GPU memory counters are identified by process/client and measured consistently. Falling resident RSS under external memory pressure is not evidence that allocations were released.

The installed nested case still includes the parent compositor's scheduling. Physical stage 8 correlates actual scanout/frame identities with these traces. A higher-refresh output needs its own budget; the 60 Hz thresholds cannot be relabelled as 120/144 Hz support. If a functional pass misses pacing, keep the functional result and the pacing failure separately.

Exit: reproducible reports identify the candidate, workloads, timings and remaining limits. Callback counts, startup screenshots and short functional runs cannot establish pacing. Actual physical scanout is measured in stage 8.

### Service contracts and fixture ownership

| Concept | Park's binding | External owner and concrete acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Bar/reserved space | Existing layer-shell usable-area/output/input policy | Chosen bar creates its own surfaces/reservation. Park excludes its reserved strip from tiled/full-width space, preserves popup/input mapping and restores space when the surface closes; no built-in bar/layout daemon |
| Launcher and terminal/browser | Existing literal argv, activation and capability-scoped native launch | Selected tool starts a native Wayland client. Foreground activation is authorized, background mapping does not steal focus, and closing the last app leaves Park alive |
| Clipboard/history and IME | Existing data-device/data-control and text-input/input-method boundaries | Chosen provider handles storage/composition. Check Unicode/preedit/candidate placement, disconnect/restart, selected editor ownership and no delivery to hidden/locked/captured fields |
| Lock and idle/power | Existing session-lock/inhibition and output/session handoffs | Chosen services decide when to lock/suspend. Park protects every output, blocks ordinary input/capture and recovers through the authorized route |
| Screenshot/sharing | Existing capture protocols plus stage-3 privacy/session state | Chosen portal/PipeWire/screenshot provider handles selection/consent/recording. Check output/window targets, cursor, resize, revoke/stop and cleanup |
| Display/accessibility/hardware controls | Existing output-management, native semantics and declared external actions | Chosen output tool/reader/brightness/audio/media command uses its capability, gets useful unavailable feedback and cannot gain unrelated privileges |
| Service lifetime | Existing ready hook and generic manager wrapper, with selected UWSM binding | Supervisor finalizes readiness/environment and cleans only Park's scope. Real host user-manager startup/teardown is accepted in stage 10 |

Each privileged helper launch gets its own authorized Wayland connection and explicitly listed capabilities. Do not share one connected socket among independent programs, authorize by executable basename/app ID, inherit Niri control sockets or implicitly give a provider capture/input/output access because it draws a bar. Reconnection/restart creates a fresh authorized connection through the same general binding. Bound outstanding work and stop/revoke the old connection's objects normally.

Use a private runtime/Cell and private D-Bus activation environment for nested compatibility. Never run a host-global `uwsm finalize` or overwrite the real user-manager `WAYLAND_DISPLAY` while Niri is active. Validate the ready adapter's literal arguments/ordering in isolation, then prove the actual manager finalization at clean login. Record the original manager display environment before/after every installed nested/provider fixture and require equality.

First use the user's selected tools, including local Noctalia modules where relevant, but keep those names out of product routing. An unsupported module/capability is reported individually. Stop only children/scopes owned by that fixture, wait/reap them and verify the existing Niri session/agents remain running. If an integration fails, identify the missing protocol/lifetime binding and correct that general boundary; do not build a substitute bar, launcher, IME, locker, portal or notification service.

Exit: installed/private-session launch, authorization, disconnect/restart, input/capture and cleanup work without overwriting Niri's environment. Each privileged helper has its own authorized connection. Record optional/unselected modules; do not implement those services inside Park. Keep actual-login checks for stage 10.

#### Recovery architecture and concrete acceptance

Keep `DesktopAction::InspectWindowRecord` → captured `WindowInspection` → existing Park `Records` intent → `full_record::open_in` as the path. The retained Area owns its UID/workspace and normal editor. `WindowRecord.creating` gates subscriptions and mutation until the matching durable profile confirmation; a later focus or app ID/title cannot choose another target. `refresh_inspections` changes creation feedback/confirmation, while the normal fresh Cell result populates that same card.

The historical v8 run passes ordinary toast dismissal, exact control activation, empty-editor typing and actual Cell Save. No placeholder Record text, programmatic diagnostic clearing, forced focus, application offset or shared editor-layout change is required. Any later shared UI correction must also retain ordinary empty-Record editing in Sandbox.

| Case | Concrete example | Pass condition |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Failed creation and retry | Open Properties for A, fail its private profile write, click Retry once, switch workspace, close A, then restore writes | Exactly one retry request, one proposed UID, one eventual new Record and the same Area/original workspace; no editor/query before confirmation and no focus theft |
| Empty body after recovery | Raw → type `created-after-close` through the visible field | Actual Cell body matches; one inspector remains; unrelated Record head/body/assertions are byte-for-byte unchanged |
| Quiet loading and deletion | Hold the real Store pool connection during the query; release it, then later delete through Engine | Same captured UID/card, unchanged input serial and changed ready-frame pixels; no pointer, key, test barrier or manual wake after setup |
| Permission failure and recovery | Withdraw access, attempt edit/Save, restore the same authorized source and click the normal recovery control | No unauthorized mutation or stale response; retained draft and same UID; ordinary typing/Save succeeds after fresh authority |
| Ordinary regression matrix | Flat/spatial × tiled/free Properties, two deletion/Refresh cases and two toolkit menu/dialog/capture cases | All nine named Qt tests, including creation recovery, pass together; no skipped/missing case accepted as a pass |

Permission recovery passes with the correction below. Keep the nine-case Qt verifier and installed/provider scope distinctions unchanged.

#### Restore permission without reusing signed Action identity

The v19 real permission run fails in 85.95 seconds after Refresh. Its retained draft, source, UID, Area and editor survive, and `InputFocus` names that editor, but the visible status reports `signed Action intent sequence is not next or message id was already used`. The captured field is actually focused. Treat this as the demonstrated live save failure, not a reason to add focus retries, application offsets or another editor layout.

The old live driver signs each `ClientMessage::Act` using its caller's reply ID as the signed intent's message ID. The shared durable autosave path retains its operation/reply ID when retrying an unacknowledged change; it currently retries after five seconds. Other ordinary callers can reuse correlation IDs between completed requests. The server requires each signed message identity to be unique for its authenticated session. Keep the server's signature, sequence and used-message checks unchanged. The implemented `live_client/actions.rs::Actions` adapter assigns each signed delivery a fresh wire ID, signs it with the next sequence, and restores the caller's original ID on terminal `ActionOk`/`Error`. Payload operation IDs remain unchanged, so existing operation receipts still prevent duplicate mutations. The driver adds no automatic retry.

| Interaction | Caller reply ID | Signed wire identity | Required result |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Edit while update authority is revoked | `body-autosave` | Fresh A, sequence 1 | Real permission refusal; stored body unchanged; reply still names `body-autosave` |
| Restore authority and edit again | `body-autosave` | Fresh B, sequence 2 | Actual save succeeds through the same source/editor; no replay conflict |
| Subsequent ordinary edit | `body-autosave` | Fresh C, sequence 3 | Same reply correlation, a distinct intent and the intended new body |
| Replay the exact earlier denied signed request | Original signed A | Reused A/sequence 1 | Server rejects `action_intent_replay` even though authority is now present; no body mutation |
| Fresh edit after that replay rejection | `body-autosave` | Fresh D, sequence 4 | Normal save still succeeds; no automatic replay, sequence reset or permission bypass |

Pending reply mappings are bounded to 128 entries with at most 512 bytes per caller ID. A local invalid ID, encoding/preparation failure or full queue cannot consume a sequence number. Terminal replies remove their mapping, unrelated updates do not, and disconnect drops remaining connection-local mappings. Use checked sequence advancement and retain failure feedback; do not retry a potentially mutating request automatically or weaken the authenticated session to recover.

Three unit regressions pass at v21 in 0.02 seconds: reused IDs, exact success/error correlation, bounded admission/no sequence gaps, unrelated/duplicate replies and exhaustion. The real-network case passes in 4.44 seconds, then its v22 extension passes in 4.53 seconds. That extension uses the same Loro `encode_update` and durable `ChangeRecord` request as native autosave: deny, retain the same payload operation ID, restore permission, retry, and deliver the successful operation again. Require one appended text change, zero duplicate facts and exactly one operation receipt. The exact old signed proof still receives `action_intent_replay`, and a fresh edit afterward succeeds. Source checks pass with warnings denied; no migration, new transport, credential UI or Park-specific authentication path is added. The original native permission fixture now passes through ordinary typing/Refresh/save in 18.15 seconds, with both actual capture states inspected; retain it in final-source regression and delivery coverage.

Exit: all recovery interactions pass through ordinary controls, including backend-only updates. Do not create another inspector, transport or properties database.

The packaged session launcher forwards literal argv, keeps an existing D-Bus session and establishes one only when missing. Isolated launcher checks passed, including the D-Bus configuration needed inside a Nix closure.
