Park's modifier-drag binding is implemented in `desktop/manipulation.rs`, `server/manipulation.rs` and `presentation/manipulation.rs`. The displayed input snapshot carries effective pointer bindings, eligible entity/placement/root/workspace/output/surface identities and the active request token. Desktop recognizes a configured application gesture and obtains Smithay's focus-clearing management grab before sending any button to the client. It respects existing grabs, popup/drag ownership, shortcut inhibition, modal ownership and output selection. The grab forwards seat bookkeeping through Smithay's inner handle while keeping client pointer focus empty. No application-name rule or new input/layout engine is involved.

The typed worker event contains the stable target, operation, optional explicit edges and matching initial/current coordinates:

```rust
Input::Manipulation(Event::Begin(Request {
    active: Active { token, target },
    operation,
    edges: None,
    start: position,
    current: position,
}), pointer_stamp)
```

`Input::Manipulation(Event::End { token, position, commit }, pointer_stamp)` resolves release/cancel only for that token. High-rate movement stays on the existing latest-pointer channel and uses the ordinary native coordinate conversion before driving the shared projection controller. An old displayed frame cannot reject a begin until its tracked input serial is acknowledged; a stale end cannot affect another gesture. Held-button quarantine is separate from the active preview, so rejection, cancellation and the initiating button's release do not leak a remaining held button. Focus/lock/layer takeover use the existing cancellation paths, while plain Escape is intercepted in the existing compositor keyboard dispatcher; no second key dispatcher or immutable modifier assumption is added. Physical/provider lifecycle acceptance remains below.

The bridge v1 combined run passes **323 shared regressions in 9.09s** and **six Park cases in 203.27s**, after **2m51s** compilation (`ergonomics-pointer-bridge-functional-v1.log`). The Park selection contains four existing presentation GPU cases, a new ordinary management-grab regression and a new private real-Wayland-client/worker GPU case. Production input code is unchanged in v2/v3; those revisions strengthen the client test and register release preparation. The current two bridge cases pass in **4.45s** after **16.24s** compilation, and test-source checking passes in **4.88s** (`ergonomics-pointer-bridge-{input,check}-v3.log`). The client test drives actual `Desktop` button/key input and worker frames, early modifier release, stale begin acknowledgements, a wrong-token end, commit, Escape restoration, a quick gesture and unmapping before begin acknowledgement. An opaque SHM buffer is sampled in the exported canvas before/after movement and cancellation. Intercepted buttons do not reach the client; a subsequent ordinary right-click delivers one press/release pair with valid input authority. The separate grab regression covers multiple held buttons, no activation authority for owned presses and seat cleanup.

The bridge-only fixture uses one flat 640×480 output and adopts acknowledged worker frames without physical scanout or the full renderer's configure/ack lifecycle. Its checkpoint does not establish CSD titlebar requests or resize acknowledgement; the later CSD fixture below adds those endpoints. Spatial/multi-output client input, installed behavior and performance still require their acceptance. The existing runner registers `window-manipulation-input` beside `window-manipulation-rendering`; registration is not an optimized pass. All **190 ordinary Park tests pass in 3.53s**, with **35 GPU cases ignored** in that ordinary run. Park/Sandbox GUI checks pass with warnings denied in **15.30s / 9.03s**, the portable interface check in **0.91s**, the server-only feature check in **38.19s**, and all **18 validation-tool tests in 0.11s** (`ergonomics-pointer-bridge-{park-ordinary,park-gui,sandbox-gui,portable,server-only,xtask}-v3.log`). The `ergonomics-pointer-bridge-v3-*` source manifest, scoped archive and copied test-executable checksums retain that checkpoint; they are not an installed GUI package.

Standard client move/resize adapters are implemented in `State`'s `XdgShellHandler` and the existing manipulation modules. The server accepts only the current Smithay implicit `ClickGrab` serial from the actual held initiating button, matching seat, live client and requested toplevel. Ordinary subsurface ancestry is accepted; popup ancestry is not treated as a toplevel press. A valid request immediately claims the existing focus-clearing management grab, then enters a 32-entry bounded queue. Further requests cannot replace that claimed interaction. Invalid/empty resize edges, stale/released/keyboard serials, foreign clients, wrong windows and wrong seats are ignored without changing the live grab. A client's own authorized CSD request is allowed while its shortcuts are inhibited; this path does not force-disable its inhibition policy as a global modifier action would.

`Server::pointer_button_serial` preserves the normal button API while returning the actual press serial to Desktop. `remember_pointer_press` retains its source surface, stable target, viewport/origin and native desktop point once. Desktop matches a queued request against that exact still-held press, quarantines held buttons, rejects changed output/view identities and sends the existing tokenized worker request with the original point plus the current point. `Server::pointer_motion` still uses surface-local seat locations; the implementation never treats `grab_start_data.location` as desktop-global. Both CSD and modifier entry points now use `start_manipulation`, the same projection/model and the same release/cancel path. No application-name special case, titlebar implementation or extra permanent control is added to Lince.

`WindowState.resizing` is an ephemeral protocol flag controlled by the shared session, including before its first motion. A resize target and affected preview items report it while owned; cancellation/commit clear it even if a physical button remains quarantined. The normal `configure_state` path sends `xdg_toplevel::State::Resizing` alongside existing fullscreen/maximize/activation state. It is not stored in workspace or interface configuration. Client requests retain exact edge masks through `begin_with_edges`; a right-edge request changes width without changing height even if the pointer also moves vertically.

The CSD v3 combined run passes **324 shared regressions in 9.15s** and **ten Park cases in 215.17s**, after **3m02s** compilation (`ergonomics-csd-functional-v3.log`). That Park selection includes six GPU cases and four ordinary protocol/grab cases. Protocol checks cover all eight resize edges and ignored None, a valid subsurface source, foreign client/wrong window/wrong seat, stale/released/keyboard serials, duplicate claims and preserved shortcut inhibition. The new real-client GPU case delays a move request until after pointer motion, checks the original-press displacement, resizes a single edge, commits, performs another resize and cancels with Escape. Each configure is submitted through the normal server path; the client acknowledges it, commits a matching opaque SHM buffer, and its pixels are checked in the exported canvas. The client sees the expected Resizing state and its restoration.

This CSD fixture is one flat 640×480 output with real protocol/configure/ack/buffer endpoints and the shared worker. It adopts acknowledged worker frames and submits their configurations directly through the normal server method; it does not run the complete output-composition/scanout loop or representative GTK/Qt/browser toolkits. Keep actual compositor-renderer, wide/narrow/spatial/multi-output, lifecycle, installed and performance acceptance below. Debug GPU checks do not close those gates.

Physics now consumes the same transient geometry as presentation. Flat `physics::synchronize` reads `canvas::presented`; spatial `topology::physics::synchronize` reads presented position, size and `presented_spatial`. Keep these reads on the existing held-body path, with the existing writeback guards; never install a temporary authored pose to make collision detection follow a drag. The common source of visible geometry is:

```rust
let item = crate::native::canvas::presented(world, entity);
let pose = crate::native::canvas::presented_spatial(world, entity);
```

Spatial compound parts retain their member identities and relative-offset cache. Position-only movement updates existing child collider transforms; dimension/shape changes rebuild geometry. Unchanged updates retain the collider entities. Entering/leaving a held pose and moving it clear stale linear/angular velocity, and changed held colliders wake affected sleeping peers. Accepted free/floating commits rebind only the changed members' Attachments to the existing GroupPose; tiled commits preserve free-canvas attachments. Cancellation removes the presentation override without rolling back unrelated physical motion. The production Topology runtime handles spatial collision poses in both 2D and 3D views; the minimal flat fallback remains axis-aligned.

Physics membership now excludes tiled, suppressed and other ineligible group members instead of reintroducing their hidden colliders through a free peer. A partially eligible group is held until all members can participate, preserving the full authored GroupPose/Attachments; it cannot pull a tiled member into simulation. Returning an individually floated item to tiling restores its saved free home before the workspace is untiled. Floating itself intentionally starts at the visible tiled rectangle, so recovery tests must exercise the actual float → retile → untile path rather than assume floating immediately restores the old free position.

Five focused regressions in `native/physics/manipulation.rs` pass in **5.06s**: free move/resize with Area scaling, rotated spatial colliders, commit/cancel and unchanged author/model/rules, zero stale velocity, stable stationary collider identities, sleeping-neighbor contact at the visible preview, compound move/resize and durable committed offsets, tiled-resize home preservation, and minimized/partially tiled group recovery. All **193 ordinary Park tests pass in 3.54s**, with **36 GPU cases ignored** there; the separate rendered selection passes **ten cases in 209.60s**, comprising six GPU and four protocol/grab cases. These are debug, isolated checks, not installed or physical acceptance (`ergonomics-preview-physics-{focused,shared,park-ordinary,rendered}-v6.log`).

The preparation runner registers `window-manipulation-physics` for the five focused tests in release mode; registration is not an optimized pass. The `ergonomics-preview-physics-v6-*` source manifest, scoped Rust/manifests/WGSL archive and copied test-executable checksums verify that source checkpoint. The corrected v6 tests pass without weakening the collision, group-metadata or home-preservation assertions.

Queued CSD claims now participate in cancellation before the press context is cleared. A validated standard request already owns Smithay's focus-clearing management grab while it waits in the server queue; there need not yet be an active worker preview. `desktop/manipulation.rs::cancel_manipulation` drains pending claims and quarantines matching held buttons using either the still-owned server grab or the captured button/serial/source/toplevel identity. The captured identity is needed after lock entry has already unset the grab. Normal request admission and cancellation share `Press::matches`. Cancellation must preserve ownership until every physical button is released, even when focus/lock cleanup clears the ordinary button set. Retain this order in the existing focus-loss/lock reset path:

```rust
self.cancel_manipulation()?;
self.server.clear_input(0);
self.buttons.clear();
```

`Desktop::focus` also cancels pending/active management gestures for native modal ownership, in addition to the existing layer/focus/lock paths. Operation's ordinary modal flag and the binding editor's keyboard-modal/capture flag are separate; both block a pending desktop gesture, while lock retains priority over either UI. Plain Operation keys use the existing native Wayland keyboard connection, so its Escape test must dispatch that connection as well as advance canvas frames. Do not replace that bridge with a second keyboard dispatcher or replay an intercepted press/release.

Queued requests also retain a permanent retirement flag after losing their press context. `Press` captures the worker input serial for its ordinary `Input::Application` focus request separately from the Smithay pointer serial used for authorization. `State::observe` considers only frames acknowledging that worker input; an older frame from another workspace cannot retire a new press. Once a qualifying frame loses the captured target/root/workspace/output, viewport, native origin or modal eligibility, retirement remains set even after the old context returns:

```rust
if frame.input_serial >= press.input_serial && !press.context(frame) {
    press.retired = true;
}
```

Retirement does not take ownership of an ordinary unclaimed application press. A subsequent validated CSD claim still enters the existing quarantine before admission rejects it; the original press context remains available for matching that claim until ordinary button cleanup. Do not clear retirement on return, compare a Wayland serial with a worker serial, or swallow every ordinary application release merely because its view changed. The new negative and positive tests cover these distinctions.

Ten rendered lifecycle tests in `server/tests/manipulation/lifecycle.rs`, `lifecycle/changes.rs` and `lifecycle/queued.rs` cover **29 context variants**: modifier/client resize cancellation through focus loss/return, actual lock-surface focus and unlock, exclusive layer takeover, Operation takeover, keyboard-owned logout confirmation, queued claims during those five takeovers, workspace changes, output resizing, unmap/remap and destruction/replacement. They also cover workspace/output leave-and-return with a queued/delayed claim, delayed claims after target remap/replacement, an older wrong-context frame preceding a valid press, and ordinary press/release pairing across a context change. Tests verify committed geometry, cleared Resizing state, additional held-button quarantine, restored client pixels and three subsequent ordinary right-click pairs with fresh input authority. Pixel checks wait until a native modal closes; the logout confirmation correctly dims the canvas while open. Configure/ack/state checks continue under the overlay without weakening the final opaque-client assertion. The connected unmap/remap variants retain geometry through the actual Record profile; that establishes same-session restoration, not durable Cell/provider restart or settings-editor draft acceptance.

Retain both red reproductions: `ergonomics-pointer-lifecycle-functional-v1.log` exposes the queued focus-loss release leak, and `ergonomics-pointer-context-functional-v1.log` exposes request revival after workspace/output round trips. Zero-output recovery is implemented in `presentation/outputs.rs`, `presentation/outputs/suspension.rs` and the existing desktop/runtime/renderer bindings. An explicitly received empty catalogue suspends display presentation; a standalone presentation that never received a catalogue retains its normal behavior. The same `Assignments` instance disconnects its outputs while retaining preferences. The Cell, live windows, canonical workspace/view identities and bounded previous internal targets remain alive. Logical layout dimensions stay intact instead of resizing applications into a fabricated 1×1 monitor. Bevy continues its normal main-world, asset extraction and render-world lifecycle; cameras regain their previous activity in First for layout, then suspend in Last before rendering. Existing inactive cameras remain inactive, and removed/replaced camera identities cannot reactivate another entity. This does not change the owner's physics/execution preferences or establish an idle-power measurement.

`Frame::dmabuf()` now returns an optional buffer. Suspended frames carry no image, output geometry, hit targets, native editor/drop target or ordinary shortcut bindings. Geometry revisions remain monotonic across disappearance and return. The compositor clears its imported canvas set and adopts the metadata frame without inventing a scanout or acknowledging an application frame callback. Native pointer/key/text/scroll input, stale output selection and manipulation starts are ignored while suspended; stale native drops use the existing cancellation flag. Host settings, window lifecycle, Records and save-aware shutdown continue. Lock ownership and its hardware-action policy remain compositor-owned. Reconnection uses the existing output assignment, target cleanup and configuration path; native keyboard focus returns only through the host's current focus/lock decision.

The actual API sequence is:

```rust
canvas.outputs(Vec::new())?;
let hidden = canvas.frame()?;
assert!(hidden.output.is_none());
assert!(hidden.dmabuf().is_none());
canvas.outputs(connected_outputs)?;
canvas.focus(native_input_focused)?;
```

`connected_outputs` comes from the compositor's real catalogue and `native_input_focused` from its current input policy; do not substitute a placeholder monitor or blindly restore focus. The native draft fixture covers empty-catalogue startup, two-output reconnect, unchanged workspace/item identities and sizes, a validated Ctrl+F3 draft, ignored stale input and successful headless save. Focus loss now reports capture cancellation only when capture is actually active; an already validated draft retains its review feedback and conflict validation. The added shared test resolves its existing W/camera-forward conflict normally before Save, preserving production rejection of duplicate bindings.

The real private Wayland-client fixture now exercises four paths: modifier resize and active client-requested resize through removal of the active output/reassignment to a survivor, a queued client resize across disconnect-all, and active client resize followed by lock and disconnect-all. Each restores two outputs with the same live Wayland surface, SurfaceId and committed size, cancels the old gesture, quarantines held releases and permits normal right-click input afterward. Its normal GLES compositor path verifies actual recovered client pixels and callback delivery; callbacks remain withheld while headless and while locked, with the returning locked output rendered black until unlock. The output-confirmation test expires without UI frames, bounds failed recovery when no connector exists and rejects an old Keep after a different connector returns. These are isolated virtual-output/protocol/renderer checks, not physical scanout or selected-locker acceptance.

The cold-persistence fixture passes both free resize and tiled move at v12 after correcting the leaked insertion outline. Its assertions cover stable Record UIDs/owner assertions and launch descriptions, retained native note drafts/Area identities and committed sizing rules/tokens, unchanged profile bytes during disposal, no restored transient preview and no automatically launched or zombie application windows/layout members. It uses ordinary loader/subscription results and stable authored identities rather than manually inserting a bridge, fabricating a creation reply or matching a saved window by title/app ID. The owner assertion query is scoped to the fixture's concept and retains its truncation guard. These passes prove saved data and teardown/reopen behavior for this offscreen fixture; they do not claim that Wayland clients survive a cold compositor process restart. Keep real-client interaction and same-session connection recovery as their own acceptance paths.

The creation request reuses the existing engine contract:

```rust
engine::actions::Action::CreateRecordDraft {
    draft: engine::record_creation::Draft {
        uid: pending_uid,
        head: "Application window".into(),
        ..Default::default()
    },
}
```

The pending UID is created once and saved before sending. Each attempt receives a fresh request correlation ID, scoped to its connection; the creation UID and payload must not change. The existing inspection retry retires that placement's earlier correlation without cancelling or duplicating the durable creation. Dispatch works from the pending-ID set with at most 64 in-flight creations, avoiding a scan of confirmed history for each frame. Application title/app ID remain display metadata, not persisted identity-recovery keys. The ordinary preview/save/Cell workflow and its user-facing recovery pass at v12; preserve the separate complete real-client, selected-provider and installed acceptance gates above and in stage 4/delivery.

#### Implemented combined client/Cell recovery fixture

The combined recovery cases extend the existing private compositor/worker fixture; they preserve v12's separate cold-durability test and the existing validation runner. Free resize and tiled move use actual XDG clients with configure acknowledgements, submitted buffers and final composition. One live Cell remains throughout each test. An editable note, an unrelated bound native Record/Area and a Configuration Sand use their normal native factories; settings and the note remain clear of the application's initial pointer target. The existing local Engine and saved application profiles supply actual Record UIDs and owner assertions.

The worker owns its Presentation and Cell channel. The **test-only** input variant in `runtime.rs` provides synchronized inspection, fixture storage setup and failure injection on that owner thread. It is absent from production builds and is not a registered command or permission. The passing combined real-client cases are in `server/tests/manipulation/tiled/recovery.rs`; separate toolkit/runtime, IME projection and delivery gaps remain above. The seam is:

```rust
#[cfg(test)]
Test(Box<dyn FnOnce(&mut Presentation) + Send>),
```

The existing worker input loop acknowledges it through the normal input serial. Observations use a bounded reply channel with a deadline. Do not add a socket, public evaluation action, global mutable hook or second worker. This seam selects the fixture's one-second `WorkspaceFile::with_settings` interval and closes its real `CellBridge.incoming` receiver; it must not implement reconnect, apply a fake creation reply or modify expected Record/geometry results. Cloning the old outgoing sender and awaiting its actual closure proves the local task stopped through the normal channel, while the compositor and application connections remain alive. A closure probe may find that the task already ended; accept its send failure only with a verified closed sender, then retain the closure deadline.

Live-cell v3 reproduces the corrected product failure: the local connection-closed message cleared Protein Area rows, removing an unrelated native Record Castle's generated binding. The shared correction is in `native/protein_area/local.rs` and its parent update/receive path. Preserve cached rows, generated entities, grouping/order and drafts for a confirmed local transport interruption, while marking the Area unavailable for mutations. Retire uncertain action correlations without replaying arbitrary actions. Track the actual local sender identity, give each replacement-channel subscription a fresh ID, retain its configured thread limits and accept only current subscription replies. Subscription messages may flush while an Area waits for fresh data; cached Record actions and bound text-editor transport stay gated. Permission/query failures, invalid rows and remote authentication failures retain their existing clearing behavior. Receive old connection messages before detecting/requeuing the replacement sender, so an old stop message cannot erase the new request. The v6 shared and combined GPU suites pass as recorded above. Preserve the failing v3 and closure-probe-race v5 evidence; neither was a passing integration checkpoint.

Preserve these implemented test steps in order when extending the fixture:

1. Wait for real application Record creation and establish the committed profile/row geometry, UID set, native draft, source Area identity and actual owner assertions. Use `interface.snapshots` for ordinary saved workspace documents; a Record Castle's bound state belongs to its saved source Area, rather than assuming it occupies the standalone `records` array.
2. Start a real free resize or tiled insertion preview. Obtain a fresh timed snapshot, distinguished from the previous saved file and marked through the normal workspace metadata path. Verify that it contains committed geometry and row membership, unchanged native content and stable application placement/Record associations, without persisting the outline. Keep the rendered preview visible and clients configured normally while waiting. Do not derive expected results from the solver under test.
3. Close the actual local channel during the preview and wait for its real stopped state/notification. Preserve owner assertions, native drafts and live XDG surfaces. Cancel the gesture through ordinary Escape/release before operating another primary-button control; opening settings or changing input ownership may cancel a gesture, and must never commit the transient preview accidentally.
4. Reveal/focus the existing Configuration Sand and activate its visible **Refresh** control through actual compositor pointer events. Resolve its measured native bounds with `topology::presentation::bounds`, then the published output raster/origin; use no hardcoded screen coordinate. The test seam may inspect the control, but must not invoke Refresh/reconnect directly. Wait for a usable replacement channel that differs from the captured old sender and for actual Record subscriptions to recover.
5. Return to the original application and verify unchanged SurfaceIds/proxies, Record UIDs, owner assertions, draft text, committed sizing/placement and authored content. Require normal configure/ack/buffer interaction, composed client pixels and ordinary context-click pairs after recovery. Do not reopen the Engine, restart the compositor or launch replacement applications to imitate survival. The existing cold Engine test remains the separate durability proof.

The combined recovery checkpoint verifies ordinary editing and retained drafts, separately from captured-editor IME. Its missing `Frame.input.editor` did not establish loss of the focused Sand's draft. The later stage 4 projection implementation and private-provider fixture below test that endpoint directly; keep their source/checkpoint provenance separate from v12 and the preceding native-tiles cases.

The requested cyclic-size interaction is also documented by [Niri's preset widths](https://niri-wm.github.io/niri/Configuration%3A-Layout.html#preset-column-widths): one action cycles configured widths, with a separate default opening width. Park uses the user's requested order and per-item horizontal-row semantics. Keep the first version to the accepted forward cycle; a reverse-cycle chord, fixed-pixel presets and separate keys for every size are not required by this review. The omitted numbered suggestion, searching existing windows through Operation, remains deferred; keep Operation's existing commands and ordinary external launcher integration without building that search.


### Inspector and gesture reference


Start with Window properties, the separately approved fifth core step. Continue through the useful families in the checklist order, reusing the completed the window-management reference input/placement transactions. Opening-rule controls depend on the stable Record target; gestures and overview transfers depend on the shared gesture owner and cancel/commit path. Each task includes its ordinary UI destination and relevant integration checks in the family coverage below and stage 4.

#### Window properties: implemented interaction and remaining acceptance

The inspection binding, shared UI and all four ordinary real Park-client edit/focus/workspace/close/save workflows are implemented and pass at v6. Pending-creation gating, inline Retry and same-card promotion are also implemented; the failed empty-editor interaction, quiet-wakeup and visible permission-recovery checks remain open with stage 4/delivery acceptance. Shared `native/layout/desktop/inspection.rs` supplies one read-only target/availability policy: bound native Records can open in Sandbox/Park, explicit Unfocus cannot recover an old target, and an unassociated owned dialog cannot borrow its parent's Record. `HostRequest::Inspect(WindowInspection)` captures the application surface/placement and originating root/workspace/position before crossing the existing bounded host/worker channel; `Input::Inspect` validates that context. `Records` retains the captured canonical UID/creation intent without removing correlated in-flight creation requests. `full_record::open_in` constructs the existing Record Castle in the captured workspace and rejects a missing destination instead of using the later active workspace. The application header is a separate `full_record::window::Metadata` component on that Castle; it refreshes only its own read-only Text when metadata changes, preserving Record editor/draft entities and their mutation destination. Window close retains the last title/app ID and reports the missing live window. No second Record store or editor is introduced.

The combined interface/Park session/test-source check passes with warnings denied at v3 in **7.80s**. All **nine Record creation/recovery tests pass in 23.19s**, including creation completing after close while preserving the correlated request and original destination. V7's connected native workflow passes in **2.08s** after **1m12s** compilation: open the first item's Record, change focus, edit owner text, close the item, change the active workspace, save an assertion through the ordinary assertion editor, then query both actual Cell Records. Only the captured UID receives the text/assertion; the unrelated Record stays unchanged. All **138 desktop tests pass in 3.37s**, both metadata/draft-isolation tests pass in **0.04s**, and all **68 shortcut/configuration/editor tests pass in 2.94s**. V7's ordinary Park suite passes **215 tests** with **74 explicitly ignored** in **23.87s**, including all nine Record checks. The five source gates pass with warnings denied: all Park features/targets **57.87s**, nested/server-tests **8.07s**, server-only tests **2.69s**, GUI `instinct,park` **52.27s** and Sandbox `instinct` **49.36s**. Both existing real Qt workflows pass in **130.46s** on the same source, retaining the window-management reference's menu/dialog/move/resize/capture/typing acceptance in flat and perspective views. They do not yet exercise the new Properties entry itself. This is not rendered application-window inspection, client DMA-BUF/release FPS or installed/physical acceptance. Preserve `ergonomics-inspection-{save,desktop,metadata,shortcuts,park,toolkit}-v7.log`, the five `ergonomics-inspection-source-v7-*.log` files, the v7 scoped source archive and frozen executable hashes. The v7 source manifest matched that accepted checkpoint; the later Properties height and keyboard changes require their own current evidence below.

The failed v3–v6 fixtures are retained as failure evidence. The corrected harness keeps Bevy's initialized World, rejects unrelated headers without unnecessary font setup, and gives the rebound shortcut an eligible application item with the typed request expectation. Save verification must use the full Record configuration's `query()` with both UID predicates and a limit of at least two; the lightweight `records_subscription` deliberately omits body/assertions, while the single-Record inspector's ordinary query has limit one. Neither can prove that the other Record was unchanged without adapting the verification query. The passing v7 check keeps both positive and negative mutation assertions. Real Park-client UI/Save, missing/deleted/unavailable Record interaction, optimized and installed acceptance remain in stage 4.

The new real-client workflow is `server/tests/manipulation/tiled/toolkit/properties.rs`. V12 passes the flat tiled case in **20.05s** after **13.08s** compilation. It opens `/window/properties` through actual Mod+P, types a Concept into the visible assertion field and commits its normal completion, switches Description to its existing Raw view, edits through native pointer/keyboard input, changes application focus and workspace, then requests ordinary Wayland close while another native character is queued. Actual Cell queries verify the original UID receives the text/assertion and an unrelated UID stays unchanged. The same editor entities and originating workspace survive, and the visible metadata reports the missing live window. This is actual UI/Cell acceptance for that projection/layout, not yet the complete four-case matrix or release acceptance.

Keep the source fixture read-only when locating controls: inspect retained bindings, widget bounds, texture dimensions and published homographies, then drive the production seat and presentation pump. Do not set focus, move the inspector or replace its input text to make this test pass. The default Description view is a preview; the v9 failure (**12.64s**) incorrectly tried its hidden Raw editor. V11's **28.75s** failure stops after the first Enter selected a real Concept completion; the fixture now observes that completion and submits it through the next Enter. Expected normal Qt process exit is permitted only after the test requests close; its child is still reaped. Private Cell/socket/config/cache boundaries remain unchanged.

V13's free-canvas case finds a product defect in **14.70s** after **13.33s** compilation: a desktop-managed native Record card keeps the initial **160-pixel height**, clipping its later controls. The rendered proof is `properties-v13/review.png`; preserve its failed log, scoped source archive and frozen executable. The shared Record row already computes a content-based height and cap, but desktop's automatic `LayoutBox` makes it Fixed before those measurements arrive. The v15 correction starts automatically managed Protein rows with a Fit height, passes their existing measured/preferred height into the shared solver, and keeps the declared minimum independent so a long scrollable Record cannot enlarge the whole tiled row implicitly. An explicit manual resize changes Fit to Fixed and remains authoritative. Height measurement updates the layout only when its value actually changes, avoiding perpetual layout/influence invalidation. Ordinary unmanaged Sandbox rows retain their existing sizing path. The new focused regression covers the content cap, unchanged minimum, stable change tick, shrinking content and retained manual size; it passes in **0.04s**. All **28 Protein-area tests pass in 22.53s** and all **138 desktop tests pass in 4.04s** on the frozen v15 executable. Source checking passes at v14 in **16.61s**; the corrected free-canvas UI reaches the previously clipped editors, but its later focus workflow still needs the acceptance below.

The unassisted v16/v17 Properties workflow then exposes a fast focus-handoff failure: after another application and a workspace round trip, a fresh Alt+Tab does not return to the native inspector. V17 fails in **60.95s** despite no held compositor keys, pending output, deferred input or modal owner. V19's extra read-only worker inspection adds an interface frame and makes that same case pass in **22.69s**; this timing-dependent diagnostic is not acceptance of the fast path. The additional inspection/frame is removed from the production-path fixture. The small CPU regression at v21 reproduces a lost fresh chord in **0.03s**, independently of Qt/GPU, after **18.88s** compilation. Preserve its red executable/source hashes and `ergonomics-properties-focus-red-v21.log`.

The implemented ordered-boundary correction keeps the existing conservative handling of an unordered host `KeyboardFocusLost`. Park already discards pre-loss keyboard messages when native input ownership ends; it now calls the shared `native::cancel_keyboard_input` at that exact boundary. The shared controller clears and acknowledges its stale keyboard/pointer/modifier state immediately, while leaving the loss notification available to shortcut capture and other readers. Fresh keys queued afterward may run in the next update without an extra frame. Bevy's delayed loss notification would otherwise release fresh held physical keys; restore only the fresh focused controller's held state after that input pass. The focused checks verify stale modifiers do not survive, fresh held modifiers do, release remains paired and capture still cancels. Do not clear every reader's notification, synthesize application input, delay focus or add a Qt-specific workaround. Debug source acceptance appears below; optimized, installed and the remaining stage 4 ownership matrix stay open.

V22's boundary regression passes in **0.03s**, with **19 native keyboard tests / 20 shortcut-editor tests / the existing Park input-cancellation test** passing in **0.31s / 2.06s / 0.03s** on frozen executables. The real four-case Properties matrix still fails in **145.72s**: both flat cases fail their unassisted MRU return, while the two spatial cases stop at the editor-focus assertion. Preserve this failed matrix rather than treating the smaller regression as complete runtime acceptance. The spatial fixture now waits for the clicked editor's published identity, without retrying the click or setting focus itself; the current immediate assertion did not distinguish delayed publication from a failed click. That change still needs actual runtime evidence.

V24's input trace reproduces the flat free failure in **59.71s** and distinguishes a second bug: the native controller retains Super after a workspace transition, while the physical seat has already released it. The old `restore_modifiers` only adds held keys; a subsequent real Alt+Tab is prepared with stale Super and cannot match the configured chord. The correction sends the complete current modifier snapshot through the existing bounded worker channel, immediately before the ordinary key that needs it. Shared `synchronize_keyboard_modifiers` compares the controller's retained state and not-yet-processed keyboard messages with that snapshot, emitting only missing presses and stale releases through the existing input path. Queued text/actions remain ordered, and an unchanged held Alt cannot acquire a synthetic release that would commit an MRU preview. Empty snapshots release stale modifiers too. Use the current XKB modifier roles rather than a fixed list of physical keys, and keep ordinary application delivery/authorization unchanged. Verify both queued pre-snapshot actions and the subsequent command, plus a still-held MRU preview, before re-running the Qt matrix.

The worker ordering is the existing focus handoff, a current modifier snapshot and the original key; the snapshot contains no keybinding or Record target:

```rust
self.send(Input::Focus(true))?;
self.restore_modifiers()?;
self.recent_key(code, pressed, repeat, &event)?;
```

`restore_modifiers` supplies `Input::Modifiers(Vec<(KeyCode, Key)>)`; the worker calls `Presentation::modifiers`, which applies the shared synchronization to its actual input window. This is an internal seat-state binding, not a new protocol, configuration table or application-specific workaround. The current toolkit runner requires all nine named Qt cases: the existing flat/spatial menu/dialog/manipulation workflows, all four Properties projections/layouts, the flat-free/spatial-tiled deletion/Refresh workflows and the new pending-creation recovery workflow below. Empty selections, the previous two-case, six-case or eight-case log alone, any missing case and any failed case must remain failures. Historical eight-case attempts retain their original scope. An absent optional Qt runner and an installed executable that the source fixture cannot exercise keep their existing explicit unrun status.

Both modifier regressions pass at v25: a queued pre-snapshot action and the following fresh command execute in order, stale Super releases, and restoring an unchanged held Alt leaves the MRU preview open. The accepted shared suites pass **21 keyboard tests / 68 shortcut tests / 139 desktop tests / 28 Protein-area tests** in **0.33s / 3.14s / 3.72s / 21.35s**; the ordinary Park suite passes **216 tests**, with **78 explicitly ignored**, in **26.68s**. V27's two metadata/draft-isolation tests pass in **0.28s**. The scoped v25→v29 checksum comparison changes only `toolkit/properties.rs`, so this production evidence remains applicable; it is not a claim that the earlier fixture validated later test code.

The perspective fixture initially used the shared flat layout snapshot to locate controls on a projected 3D card. V26's read-only failure diagnostic proves that its click reached a different actual native field; preserve that **53.99s** failure. The locator now uses the compositor's actual presented `Frame.geometry`, waits for the Record card to appear there, and chooses a visible interior point when a control's center is clipped. It does not set focus, move the camera/item, retry a failed click or replace text. V27's two old Qt workflows pass, but that combined run fails its four Properties cases because their card was not yet presented when sampled (**188.42s** overall). V28 passes three Properties cases and fails the tiled perspective case after choosing an offscreen center for the Raw field (**96.69s** overall). These earlier fixture corrections alone did not establish camera reveal. The later v6 implementation passes the native suite and full fitting-card Qt assertion; stage 4 retains its expanded runtime/optimized/installed acceptance.

All **four Properties workflows pass together at v29 in 89.87s**, after **16.12s** compilation. They use actual Operation input, native assertion completion and Raw description controls, an unassisted MRU/workspace round trip, ordinary client close with a queued final edit and actual Cell queries for the intended and unrelated UIDs. All retain the original editor/workspace and show the missing live-window metadata after close. The two original flat/spatial Qt menu/dialog/manipulation workflows pass individually in v27; the failed combined report remains a failure rather than a six-case pass. This is debug source-fixture correctness with a software-rendered Qt Wayland client and hardware compositor, not release performance, client DMA-BUF, installed/provider or physical-session acceptance.

The five source gates pass with warnings denied at v27: all Park features/targets **22.31s**, nested/server-tests **7.32s**, server-only tests **1.60s**, GUI `instinct,park` **15.25s** and Sandbox `instinct` **12.52s**. The final test-only correction also passes the all-feature/all-target source gate at v29 in **3.23s**. All **12 validation-runner regressions pass in 0.07s** after **5.81s** compilation, including every named Qt case, each possible omission/failure, an empty selection and the old two-case-only report. Preserve the scoped `ergonomics-properties-v29-{source.sha256,rust-source.tar,archive.sha256}` and executable checksum, the v25/v27 frozen executables and the named check/test logs. Current scoped source, archive and executable checksums verify; the scoped archive omits assets/toolchains/owner data and is not an installable package.

Availability/retry work now extends that accepted checkpoint; the v29 checks do not validate these later changes. The new shared code is `protein_area/availability.rs`, its focused tests and `record_binding/recovery.rs`. The red v1 reproduction passes an empty result to an existing single-Record inspector and loses its actual draft/editor entity in **0.04s** after **1m41s** compilation. Preserve `ergonomics-availability-red-v1.log`, its frozen executable and scoped source/checksums. V3's four availability regressions pass in **0.12s** after **4m08s** compilation: deletion/unavailability retains the draft and UID, permission refresh preserves the same editor, old subscription responses cannot restore it, another returned UID is rejected, and ordinary multi-Record views still remove unmatched rows. These are focused model/UI-construction checks, not yet rendered error/retry acceptance.

The implemented availability path recognizes an enabled single-Record query with a canonical UID and limit one. It retains that inspector's original row when data disappears, clears authoritative query results and hides unavailable fields while preserving draft/editor entities. Inline status and an ordinary **Refresh Record** widget retain the captured target; fresh results restore the same entities. Status/metadata remain directly under the row so data-layout changes cannot hide them. The cached target/panel avoids a World search per Area/frame. Both actual deletion/Refresh workflows pass at v6; visible permission recovery remains open.

Refresh captures that same area/UID/source, validates it against the current configuration and uses the existing Cell/Organ transport. It allocates a fresh subscription ID, retires the old one and keeps a bounded in-flight flag so repeated clicks do not enqueue replacements. Ignore old subscription results and reject a different UID even when its response uses the current subscription ID. Reuse the existing Organ login form inside the retained status when authentication is required; discard that temporary form after authentication and wait for fresh Record data. This adds no desktop-login or account service. A failed collaborative text binding retries only within the same authenticated principal: keep its CRDT document, undo state and durable request IDs, discard old connection state and require a fresh join/write-authority response. A login change cannot resume the previous login's pending writes. The initial v3 binding test used an invalid placeholder owner and hit the ownership guard. The corrected valid-owner checks pass at v4, and all **13 binding tests pass in 17.00s** at v5, including fresh join correlation and rejection of a retired same-UID authority reply. Actual restored typing/Save remains a separate interaction gate.

At availability v6, all **73 Protein-area tests pass in 22.81s**, including inline Organ reauthentication and hiding/restoring authoritative `RecordProperties`. The broader checks pass **139 desktop / 68 shortcut / 2 metadata / 216 ordinary Park tests** in **3.63s / 3.41s / 0.05s / 28.03s**; 80 Park cases remain explicitly ignored there. Five warnings-denied source gates pass: all Park features/targets **57.40s**, nested/server-tests **7.53s**, server-only tests **1.65s**, GUI `instinct,park` **53.64s**, and Sandbox **51.69s**. All **12 validation-runner tests pass in 0.07s**, requiring the new eight-case inventory and rejecting a six-case-only report.

That eight-case Qt run **fails: five pass and three fail in 254.73s**. All four ordinary Properties cases and the old flat workflow pass; both new deletion cases cannot find a displayed Refresh control, and the old spatial workflow times out waiting for owned-dialog focus. Inspected deletion captures show a blank black native card even though its internal status text is set. Do not equate a `Text` value or `Node.display` flag with rendered feedback. V7 adds a read-only wait for the actual projected Refresh geometry and node/scroll diagnostics, without setting focus, moving the card or retrying a failed click. Its first link fails; the shared `target` directory disappears during the retry, removing the saved logs, scoped archives, executables and captures. Treat the results above as historical observed outcomes, not currently inspectable artifacts. V8 regenerates the scoped source archive and compilation; rendered error/retry and dialog-focus acceptance remain open until the actual corrected workflow passes.

V8 does not reach its tests: concurrent Engine/source-library work changes the manifest and source during dependency compilation. The observed Engine failures include a missing declared module, an unresolved newly added dependency/methods and an uncovered action variant. Its job terminates with eight errors. V9 then refuses the temporarily unsynchronized workspace lockfile under `--locked`; its tests also do not run. Preserve these as source/build failures, not rendered recovery results. V10 uses Cargo's normal offline resolution against the current shared workspace and regenerates its scoped source/archive before validation; it must still pass the locked source gates after the concurrent changes settle. Do not revert another task's Engine, document, Sandbox or workspace changes to force this fixture to compile.

V10 terminates before the interface tests compile: Engine has an unused `store::sqlx::Row` import and omits the already-dispatched `Action::Sources` variant from its exhaustive fallback match. The narrow build correction removes that unused import and adds the handled variant to the existing unreachable group, preserving source-library behavior. V11 then fails during live dev-shell evaluation because the concurrent PDF change references an undefined `pdfium` package. A dependency shell reconstructed from the recorded v10 flake evaluates successfully at v13, using the same pinned live inputs and the explicit Git-backed source reference; no checkout, package implementation or runtime code is replaced. Its profile is `target/park-validation/interface-env-v10`. V14 uses that dependency environment to compile/test the current shared source and initially waits for the other live Cargo check's build lock. Keep the live flake's evaluation/package gate open: testing Park in the recorded dependency environment cannot prove delivery of the newer PDF dependency or the installed session. Capture the final actual source again after concurrent edits settle; the earlier scoped archives must not be labelled current when their hashes differ.

V14 reaches the interface and fails before running tests: concurrent video/source changes add APIs while the already compiled dependencies still lack them, two calls use `ClientMessage::Action` instead of the existing `Act` variant, an import is unused, two video fixtures omit the new settings fields, and one raw copyright byte makes the embedded PDFium notice invalid UTF-8. The mechanical fixes use the existing protocol variant, remove the unused import, initialize `resource: None`/`muted: false` in those fixtures and transcode the one invalid copyright byte without changing the notice's text. The source-library/video APIs are now present in the shared source and need a fresh dependency compilation, not duplicate implementations. The live flake's PDF dependency has meanwhile been repaired through `pdfiumPackage`; its dev shell evaluates successfully. The concurrent native-interface library check passes with warnings denied in **18m20s**, but does not compile test-only code or establish the Park/session/source gates. V15 returns to the live `.#interface` shell, aligns native compilation with `CFLAGS=-g0`, and runs the dedicated test build after the shared Cargo lock becomes available. It has no accepted runtime result yet. The v15 scoped manifest/archive also includes the literal embedded PDFium notice, while remaining a source checkpoint rather than a complete installable package.

Require the deletion fixture to prove that **Refresh actually performed a new query**. Merely clicking its rectangle and seeing the same unavailable message is insufficient: that message already existed before the click. The fixture now installs a bounded, passive reader of actual `CellMessage::Snapshot` replies before opening the inspector, records the original captured-UID subscription, then requires a distinct fresh empty Record-query response after the normal pointer click. It also waits for loading to complete, keeps the original UID/editor/draft and verifies the unrelated Record through the actual Cell. The reader observes replies without changing transport, focus, input, Record state or query results; no test-only production API or fabricated response is introduced. A missed button or no-op Refresh must time out. Preserve the separate actual rendered-control/pixel checks and the current nine-case Qt inventory, including creation recovery.

Also verify the idle update path before full runtime acceptance: a backend-only loading/error/deletion change must wake normal presentation and display its settled status without requiring a mouse move, another key or repeated test-forced frames. The toolkit's explicit frame pump remains useful for locating a layout failure, but cannot by itself prove that the ordinary desktop schedules enough subsequent layout/render work after the triggering message. Observe the real worker/wake/publication path while the client is quiet and preserve the existing bounded redraw behavior; do not solve it with a permanent render loop. Keep this with the pending visible loading/error/retry checks and stage 4's quiet-workspace acceptance.

The initial-loading implementation uses the same Record-row factory and placement path as a loaded inspector. Before the first result, the captured canonical UID owns one runtime loading row with null row data, no authoritative `RecordProperties`, and no editable Record fields. Its inline status distinguishes loading, unavailable and authentication/error feedback; it does not claim a saved draft exists before one has loaded. A matching first result populates that same card entity and removes the loading marker, without creating another Record or stealing focus. A failed or retired response cannot promote the shell into authoritative data. Only a single-target Record-source query is eligible; generic multi-Record views retain their existing lifecycle. Application metadata remains a separate read-only section and survives unavailable data. The implementation also excludes that metadata/status from custom field-presentation rearrangement and skips presentation updates while the Record is missing, so changing a field layout cannot hide Refresh or reveal stale editors. The `availability/loading.rs` checks pass for the no-data boundary, same-card arrival, initial permission/retry correlation and custom-presentation behavior. Actual initial-loading presentation and quiet-client wakeup remain separate acceptance work.

The v15 test compilation is deliberately interrupted with exit 130 before producing results after a concrete input defect is found: Refresh has an `ActionButton` but no Bevy widget `Button`, while the shared action controller responds to `Activate` events from that widget behavior. V16 adds the existing `sand::button(0)` bundle and ordinary padding/minimum height to this control, giving it normal pointer/keyboard activation and tab participation. This uses the shared control implementation; do not globally attach button behavior to every `ActionButton` or special-case a client. V16 continues the dedicated build in the live dev shell. The stronger actual-response fixture remains the gate for accepting the click, not direct invocation of `Refresh::apply` in a model test.

The snapshot boundary is implemented: `canvas_host/snapshot.rs` now serializes the owning inspector through the existing native Area component/settings serializer and owned-field validation. It retains the complete nested query, Record source and UID for both local and Organ sources; the generated loading row receives no separate saved placement identity. The direct-UID shortcut is removed, while the earlier `RecordPlacement`/builtin-Record path is preserved. Focused capture/serialization tests and all **40 canvas-host tests pass at v17 in 14.87s**. Preserve that implementation rather than adding another query parser. Actual cold/runtime reload remains stage 4 acceptance; serialization alone does not establish it.

V16 terminates before tests because the linker wrapper cannot allocate scratch space. The dedicated run moves its own scratch directory into `target/park-validation/tmp` and resumes through the live dev shell. V17 passes the nine availability tests and all **13 Record-binding tests in 30.58s**, but both real deletion cases fail: Record layout reparents the status and metadata under a data-actions section that becomes hidden. The v18 correction keeps this chrome directly under the Record row; hierarchy assertions supplement the earlier display-flag checks. Both real-client deletion/Refresh cases then **pass together in 36.41s**, requiring a distinct fresh Cell response after the visible button click and retaining the original editor/draft. Their captures were inspected. The original spatial Qt menu/dialog/manipulation workflow also **passes individually in 69.41s**. This does not yet establish a combined eight-case pass or complete perspective reveal; the spatial inspector remains partially clipped. Evidence is `ergonomics-availability-toolkit-v18.log`, `ergonomics-availability-spatial-toolkit-v18.log` and `availability-v18/` under `target/park-validation`.

The full Protein-area fixture now waits for actual Record data and a populated editor rather than treating the new loading row as a loaded Record. The v19 warnings-denied test compilation completes in **3m43s**, and **all 77 Protein-area tests pass in 52.65s** (`ergonomics-availability-functional-v19.log`). These include the nine availability/loading/snapshot checks. Retain the earlier failed runtime reproductions; the focused and individual runtime passes do not replace the remaining source gates, combined toolkit run, optimized or installed acceptance.

Remaining Window properties acceptance is quiet backend-only loading/status wakeup, visible permission recovery, and affected native/Record/nine-case Qt checks after the shared layout correction. V8 now passes all nine Qt workflows, including corrected toast/Raw interaction, post-recovery typing/Save and final isolation assertions. V9 diagnoses the loading card's repeated layout wakes. Run the final source gates and repeat the real matrix on the corrected source; retain cold reload, optimized, mixed-DPI, installed/provider and physical acceptance separately. Stage 1 owns these actual gaps.

The quiet-wakeup fixture described here has not yet been written or run; it is an acceptance gap, not evidence of a demonstrated wakeup defect. Use the real `CanvasWorker::spawn_notifying`, connected local Cell and hardware-exported frames. `CanvasWorker::frame()` consumes a published result without waking the worker; `Input::Test` is allowed only for initial setup and passive observation installation. After setup, send no barrier/probe/input messages, force no `Presentation::frame`, move no pointer and ring no test-created wake signal to obtain a backend update. Keep physics/animated examples off and text input unfocused so real quiescence is observable. Preserve existing wake/redraw mechanisms unless this actual workflow demonstrates a missing binding; do not add a permanent render loop.

Use the existing single-connection in-memory Store pool to delay an ordinary query after worker startup: retain its connection, open the normal inspector during setup, then observe its loading card and a quiet frame stream. Releasing the connection lets the original Cell query complete through its unchanged response/wakeup path. Require visible loaded content, the same captured UID/card and a changed rendered status region, with no new input serial. Once quiet again, delete the Record through the actual Engine and require the existing unavailable feedback to become visible through backend notifications alone. Passive Bevy observers may report state and actual measured widget bounds; they cannot deliver held replies or change UI state. Read pixels only from worker-published ready DMA-BUF frames. Check that the frame stream settles again after each transition, so a permanent render loop cannot masquerade as a working wakeup adapter. Keep this software/GPU fixture distinct from physical input latency and release FPS acceptance. No additional production diagnostic API or configuration store is required.

The implementation reuses `DesktopAction::InspectWindowRecord`, the registry's `/window/properties` entry, `Presentation::inspect_window`, `Records::inspect`/`take_inspection`, and `native::full_record::open_in`. The Park path captures a placement identity and intended UID while its Record is being created. It now opens the retained normal Castle immediately, gates its subscription/editing on confirmation and populates that same card from fresh Cell data. The shared full-Record editor and its assertion/draft mutation path remain the owner. The old `application_focus.or(last_geometry.focused)` selection fallback is removed. The remaining stage 2/UI and stage 4 gates do not require another inspector.

Use the implemented target selection through the current shared focused-item identity and existing `protein_area::RecordBinding`. Eligible native items with a Record use that binding's UID and `Source` in Sandbox and Park; an unbound native item reports the action as unavailable rather than manufacturing a Record. Park's pending application creation continues through the existing captured placement/UID path. Preserve explicit Unfocus, and do not fall back to an old compositor focus or an arbitrary nearby item when the shared current selection is absent. Reuse the existing command ID, availability/help, shortcut editor and initially unbound default.

Capture the Record source/UID and originating root/workspace when the command begins. While application creation is pending, retain the placement identity and creation intent, then resolve only its confirmed UID. A later focus/output/workspace change must not choose another Record or silently open the delayed result in another workspace. Use the existing missing/unavailable state or creation retry for a failed target. Once opened, the editor owns its captured Record binding independently of the application's entity lifetime; closing the window may remove live metadata, but does not retarget a valid Record editor or its Save. Preserve the existing draft when permission, source availability or loading fails.

The implemented application-creation UI shows a retained pending inspector in its captured workspace rather than waiting invisibly for `take_inspection` to receive an acknowledgement. It reuses the ordinary loading card, read-only metadata and inline retry control. Before confirmation, no Record query, authoritative `RecordProperties` or editable fields may be activated merely because the proposed UID is valid or already exists. Confirmation promotes that same inspector. A saved creation dependency prevents runtime-state loss after reload from enabling a query. V6 exercises pending workspace retention and same-card recovery after application closure; snapshot/gating tests pass at v3. Full cold-runtime reload and the post-recovery empty-editor correction remain acceptance work.

Retry targets the saved placement/creation UID through `Records::retry` and the existing idempotent `CreateRecordDraft` receipt. It clears only the relevant recoverable error and preserves correlated in-flight requests, the bounded creation queue and the already durable intent. Do not choose the currently focused application, allocate another UID, launch an application or let repeated clicks enqueue duplicate work. The actual Cell fixture must cover an unavailable/failed profile write, recovery with the same UID, creation finishing after the original window closes, focus/workspace changes while pending, and a contradictory or retired reply. Keep these feature/UI checks separate from the already passing post-creation deletion/Refresh workflow and the installed/provider acceptance.

The implemented pending-creation path adds `Config.window_record: Option<WindowRecord>` to the existing Area settings, serialized by the same native Area/snapshot path. `WindowRecord` stores placement, canonical UID, bounded captured title/app ID and a `creating` dependency. Validation requires a matching local, single-Record query; a different UID, remote source, multi-result query or zero placement cannot borrow this association. Read-only `Metadata` is reconstructed from those settings after reload. The runtime `Creation` component contains only Pending/Failed/Unavailable feedback and one retry-request bit; losing that component cannot clear the saved dependency or start a query. Sandbox can display a restored pending card and explain the missing Park connection while leaving its query disabled.

`Records::take_inspection` emits the captured intent once rather than waiting for its acknowledgement. `Presentation::refresh_inspections` validates the saved placement/UID, consumes the bounded retry bit through `Records::retry`, updates only creation feedback and clears `creating` only when that profile confirms the same UID. A profile-write failure, disconnected Cell or changed association remains visible; a retry also releases the existing profile-write backoff without replacing its UID or in-flight correlations. The ordinary metadata refresh may still report a live application during creation. Confirmation reconnects the existing Protein-area state in place, preserving its loading row, then waits for a fresh actual Cell read before creating editable fields. Bound mutation and editor-sender checks also reject a still-creating configuration.

The pending row also withholds the ordinary `RecordBinding` component until that fresh read. It carries only a read-only `WindowRecord` display association; the inline action retains its private captured retry target. This prevents ordinary row-click Record events or generic native Properties actions from opening the proposed UID through another inspector before creation is confirmed. `protein_area::row_area` preserves the generated row's native authoring/snapshot owner without using an authoritative binding, and the normal loading-row retention path uses the private status-panel target. Fresh data adds the normal binding and removes the temporary display association on the same row. Losing runtime state after reload still cannot start a query.

1. Shared command eligibility resolves the currently focused eligible item, permits a canonical native `RecordBinding`, and requires Park for application associations. Once accepted, pending inspection retains the intended Record UID across creation, focus/workspace changes and closure. Retrying clears a prior error without discarding correlated in-flight requests or allocating another UID. Do not pair a generic action with a separate mutable target queue.
3. `full_record::open_in` constructs the Area with captured workspace membership and position. Ordinary `open` delegates using its current-workspace context. Park refreshes title/app ID and live state by that identity; the shared header updates only its own text when metadata changes. A later focused window cannot replace those fields or the editor's binding. Close retains the valid Record editor/draft, last client identity and an unavailable live-window state. Reuse existing missing/loading/permission feedback for the Record itself.
5. Preserve the passing shared eligibility/target capture and native/real-Wayland visible-inspector Save checks. Extend delayed creation, failure/retry and unavailable/deleted/permission interaction at the mutation destination, then carry them into stage 4's optimized/mixed-DPI/installed matrix. The displayed title alone never establishes the mutation target. The stage 4 checkbox remains the acceptance gate.

Keep the shared eligibility and native entry point beside `native/layout/desktop`'s existing action/availability helpers. A valid bound native Record can open in Sandbox; application inspection requires Park's host/Cell association. Inspection captures a canonical Record UID, not a mutable slug. The ordinary full-Record opener may retain its existing slug navigation for other callers. Reject a missing binding/association explicitly; never manufacture a Record to make the inspector appear available. A parent-owned transient without its own association is unavailable rather than silently inspecting its parent.

The host request carries only the application selection context, with no Bevy or native Record-source dependency in the host model:

Keep the inspection's intended UID alongside its creation request; `take_inspection` may resolve only that UID, even if the live surface moves, closes or another creation finishes first. Clearing a prior failure permits retry of the existing intent but cannot remove an in-flight request or allocate a replacement Record UID.

Application metadata belongs to the opened inspector Area, associated with its captured placement and UID. The implemented read-only header is separate from owner-editable fields. Native bound items use the same full-Record factory without a fictitious application header. The four full-Record/metadata tests and ordinary Qt Properties workflows pass at v6. #### Desktop gestures: implementation boundary after Window properties

Desktop action routing and physical support remain unaccepted.

The pinned Smithay 0.7 general client binding is now written: `wayland::pointer_gestures::PointerGesturesState::new::<State>(&display.handle())`, `delegate_pointer_gestures!(State)` and pointer-handle swipe/pinch/hold methods in `server/gestures.rs`. `desktop/gestures.rs` and `session.rs` forward physical streams with device ownership, activity notification and cancellation, preserving `session/devices.rs::Devices::gesture` deferral until End. Three real Wayland-client protocol cases are written for complete delivery, malformed/leave cancellation and lock isolation. Compilation/execution of that new binding still needs acceptance. Ordinary scrolling through `nested/input.rs::Desktop::axis` and its application/popup/layer/native ownership remain unchanged.

Reuse the recognizer before update coalescing and current view/model/actions after delivery. Do not add another device registry, recognizer, scroll state, viewport database or renderer.

Keep client and desktop lifetimes separate. Unclaimed streams use Smithay's normal pointer-gesture path with grabs/lock respected; desktop-owned cancellation cannot become a late client Begin. Never synthesize mouse presses or keyboard chords as a gesture adapter. Normal input afterward, output/device removal, backpressure, cancellation and saved-settings restart must pass through the complete route. Sandbox/nested hosts without such a stream retain keyboard equivalents and unavailable feedback; they never open global libinput. Actual touchpad behavior remains stage 8 evidence.

### Integrated-validation reference


Input acceptance must include a key arriving in the release frame of a click that opens Overview, Operation, a width modal or binding capture, from both a real Wayland application and a native Sandbox text editor. Park's tracked handoff uses `KeyboardModalTrigger` rectangles; the native capture buffer currently recognizes the narrower `CaptureTrigger`. Verify the general ownership transition rather than assuming the capture-only fixture proves every modal. Preserve IME composition and held-key quarantine, and deliver permitted buffered text only to the new owner without replaying already-read raw keyboard messages. Operation and Window properties now join the existing typed-action barrier in `nested/input.rs`; test rapid typing and asynchronous inspector readiness through the actual runtime bridge. The shortcut GPU regression also keeps a capture button stationary while the pointer is held; focus-reveal scrolling must wait until release rather than move a pressed control away from its pointer.

the window-management reference's actual Qt workflows open an owned dialog from its own controls, type into it, close it and verify parent focus/text recovery. The release-serial activation adapter and its ten protocol/security tests cover the missing general handoff. V31/v32 also passes delayed dialog close while native settings/capture retains the keyboard, and both broader Qt workflows pass again at v6. Preserve those completed interactions; only their optimized, mixed-DPI, provider/installed extensions remain. No application-name exception or relaxed background-map authorization is permitted.

The native editor's end-of-quarantine behavior is implemented and verified below: after the last swallowed held key is released, a later permitted fresh key reaches the restored owner once. Retiring quarantine cannot replay the captured chord, turn its release into a client click or leave an unrelated new chord blocked solely because the batch began with a swallowed key. Keep the remaining real-client/IME and lock/runtime handoffs in the integrated acceptance below.

The native editor part now processes interception per event instead of retaining one blocked flag for an entire batch. Save/Cancel quarantine ends on the matching final swallowed release, and a later fresh key retains its normal physical pressed state and enters the existing prepared-event dispatcher once. Enter validates the candidate present at that event; later keys cannot overwrite it before validation. Plain Escape applies the owning modal's existing cancel action at its event, preserving IME precedence. These transitions temporarily publish the current Editor resource so ordinary actions and `release_keyboard` see the correct held-key ownership:

```rust
world.insert_resource(editor);
actions.run(world, target);
editor = world.remove_resource::<Editor>().unwrap_or_default();
```

Discard any earlier, not-yet-forwarded prefix when its modal is cancelled or its capture owner changes; it must not be replayed into the restored canvas. Keep only the permitted tail for `keyboard::replace`, and reset only still-swallowed physical keys rather than every held key. Activation-frame buffered input may continue after validation/cancellation when capture was actually confirmed; buffered input for an unconfirmed activation remains blocked. Retain the shared capture and action paths, not a second modal dispatcher or event replay engine.

The red v1 tests reproduce both defects: the fresh key after quarantine was dropped, and a later B replaced the F2 candidate before Enter validation (`ergonomics-handoff-interface-red-v1.log`). Three corrected unit cases cover Save/Cancel, retained physical state, immediate Enter validation, modal Escape, prefix isolation and no later replay. All **188 selected shared tests pass in 10.66 seconds** on v2 and **10.80 seconds** on the current v3 (`ergonomics-handoff-interface-{v2,v3}.log`). The actual pointer-driven GPU shortcut fixture also queues F2 down/up, Enter down/up and fresh B down/up in the capture click's release frame, asserting the F2 draft and exactly one ordinary B delivery. Both current shortcut/width rendered scenarios pass in **49.18 seconds** (`ergonomics-handoff-gpu-debug-v3.log`), including their wide/narrow controls; the current 360-pixel shortcut capture was visually inspected. All **177 ordinary Park tests pass in 3.34 seconds**, with the other **23 hardware/GPU tests explicitly ignored in that ordinary run**. Both current GUI checks pass with warnings denied: **11.91 seconds** for Park and **8.61 seconds** for Sandbox (`ergonomics-handoff-{park,sandbox}-gui-v3.log`). Real-client, IME, lock/inhibition and asynchronous inspector/runtime handoffs remain separate unfinished acceptance.

The unchanged current source passes `ergonomics-handoff-v3-source.sha256` after the checks. A scoped Rust/manifests/WGSL snapshot is retained as `ergonomics-handoff-v3-rust-source.tar` with its checksum, and `ergonomics-handoff-v3-test-executables.sha256` identifies the tested executables. Perspective tiled focus/reveal is implemented through the existing shared solver transaction. `tiling::shift_camera` translates the active root's `View.position` or an inactive workspace's stored `topology.position` by the actual logical center delta, preserving height, yaw, pitch, plane and selection depth. It also retains the dormant spatial anchor while 2D is visible; without an existing camera, ordinary creation initializes from the updated canvas. Leaving tiling applies the inverse translation and preserves the saved free view. This uses the same path for native and application items in Sandbox/Park, with no Record-specific focus action.

The existing `Runtime.fullscreen_views` now stores private `FullscreenView { mode, canvas, scroll, size }` values captured at entry. Exiting fullscreen restores the exact saved center/zoom when geometry is unchanged, and recalculates only for changed layout/scroll/output size or an invalidated ordinary view. It compares the entry values with the current solver, not the separate cache updated during fullscreen. Apply the spatial delta after saved-canvas restoration:

```rust
let previous_center = canvas.center;
canvas.center = resolved_center;
if !normalized && mode != WorkspaceLayout::Free {
    tiling::shift_camera(world, root, active, previous_center, canvas.center);
}
```
