#### Captured-editor projection: implemented boundary and remaining acceptance

This is a prerequisite for stage 4's real compositor/editor/IME handoff. The old final-output-image guard excluded native Sand cameras, and the old validation passed the received caret back into `text_state`, so it could not validate a changed projection. The current implementation corrects both boundaries in `native_input/projection.rs` and the ordinary frame assembly. It reuses the native text-input client and existing provider, retaining bounded text, Unicode/selection, protected-field, focus and pending-edit checks. The remaining interaction matrix above stays open; compilation or ordinary typing is insufficient acceptance.

The free-canvas Properties locator failure is corrected: both flat/spatial cases pass at v6 with published geometry and widget bounds read in one passive worker callback, and v8 repeats the complete nine-case Qt workflow successfully. Preserve strict editor identity, actual Cell mutations and normal pointer input; no offset, forced focus/camera or extra production frame was added. The distinct creation-recovery failure was a toast intercepting Raw, diagnosed at v7 and corrected through ordinary visible dismissal at v8. Retain its inspected v8 capture and exact activation/typing/Save checks; do not revive a speculative empty-editor layout correction or rely on the removed v6 capture.

Preserve the implemented sequence and its ownership boundaries:

1. Compute the final `frame_geometry()` snapshot once before building the ordinary input snapshot, then share that same immutable snapshot with `Frame.geometry` and native-editor capture. It already contains the visible Native item identities, perspective homographies, clipping and final output placement used for application pointer routing. Do not substitute the earlier rectangular-only model publication or independently derive another camera projection. The suspended path continues to publish no editor.
2. Preserve the direct-final-image editor path and its `RasterMapping.logical_rectangle` conversion. For a different image, resolve the editor's `ComputedUiTargetCamera`, that camera's `VisualOwner`, and the owner's actual `Surface`. Require matching camera/image identities, an eligible live native owner, a bounded ancestor chain from the editor to that owner, and the owner's existing layout identity in the selected output's published geometry. Reject an arbitrary offscreen image, application texture, hidden/suppressed/removed owner, wrong output or unprojectable target. Image inequality alone must never become permission to expose text.
3. Form the caret from Parley's IME area plus actual cursor geometry, retaining the positive-area normalization. Apply the node's content origin and current `TextScroll`, then its `UiGlobalTransform` to all four corners in source-image pixels. Normalize through actual `Surface.pixels` and the published surface extent, including capture-density rounding; do not assume density 1 or use the final monitor scale as the Sand's texture density. Bound the result through source/UI clipping, the published `ContentMapping`, the item's visible clip and the output work region. A fully hidden field or owner has no endpoint. A partially visible, focused authored field retains IME when its caret reaches beyond the capture/output: clamp the candidate hint to a visible edge, preserving positive bounds. Do not resize authored text areas to fit a tile or disable composition merely because the caret is cropped. The 360-pixel fixture reproduces a 216-pixel authored field in a 168-pixel tile; its caret lies beyond the image even though the field remains visible and keyboard-focused. The final published mapping already carries output translation/raster handling, so the captured path applies neither a second time. Legacy single-viewport geometry now translates clip polygons along with the mapping; explicit output targets supply their exact stored origin.
4. `CaptureState` keeps the selected output/view, actual source identity and last typed `Context` ephemerally. `Editor.projection: u64` changes when that context changes; semantic text `revision` and `Pending.ime` stay separate, so camera movement does not by itself change an edit's input-method cause. `prepare` updates the actual view before the App update, and capture uses the final published frame after it. Before applying an edit, `current` checks the captured generation and resolves the live owner/source, sensitivity, owner/root workspace, selected view, clipping, node/scroll transform and relative geometry through the existing output geometry function. A cached token alone is insufficient. This state is not persisted, exposed as authority or another desktop model. Compare only the target's context: unrelated client motion cannot continuously cancel typing, and a moving item with zero-relative-lag camera follow must remain editable. Free-editor v8 verifies unchanged caret, projection generation and provider serial during simultaneous followed-item and unrelated-client movement in both projections. Different simulation/render rates, Area changes and physical monitor handoff retain their separate stage 4 acceptance.
5. Keep the current native text-input client, input-method provider and bounded `NativeEdit` queue as the transport. A source/output/focus change retires stale requests and clears preedit through the existing cancellation path. Normal binding capture retains priority and must not expose an editor endpoint or deliver IME text to its shortcut field. Preserve standalone/native modal editors as well as captured Sands; the existing editor-drop validation remains its own bounded transaction until captured drops are explicitly integrated.

The frame assembly should share one snapshot along this shape, preserving the separate suspended branch:

```rust
let geometry = self.frame_geometry().map(Arc::new);
let input = self.input_snapshot(geometry.as_deref());
```

For each source corner, the captured mapping uses the existing source and destination extents. This illustrates the conversion after source/visibility validation, not a replacement for those guards:

```rust
let source = std::array::from_fn(|axis| {
    item.mapping.surface.origin()[axis]
        + f64::from(point[axis]) / f64::from(surface.pixels[axis])
            * item.mapping.surface.size()[axis]
});
let projected = item.mapping.canvas_point(source)?;
```

The root-scale correction belongs in the shared native renderer, not a Park-only panel offset. A `GhostNode` replaces the ordinary canvas `Node`, so Bevy no longer maintains its computed layout metadata. The old synchronizer updated its size and copied render-target information but left its inverse scale stale. The 125% reproduction records root inverse 1, child/window scale 1.25 and panel right offset 518 logical units, clipping the left edge. V6 retains physical dimensions and copies the inverse scale from the Bevy-managed toolbar belonging to this root, producing inverse 0.8 and the correct 412-unit right offset. It also maintains `unrounded_size` and matches the toolbar by its `ChildOf(root)` association, avoiding another canvas's target/density. Keep the metadata consistent through scale changes and reconnect, without hardcoded DPI ratios or a separate panel-layout policy:

```rust
node.size = physical_size.as_vec2();
node.content_size = node.size;
node.unrounded_size = node.size;
node.inverse_scale_factor = toolbar.inverse_scale_factor();
```

The v6 root regression covers 100/125/150/200% density transitions and restoration, complete physical bounds and an independently scaled second canvas. It passes after **2m28s** compilation (`ergonomics-editor-handoff-root-v6.log`). All **19 edit-mode tests pass in 9.89s** and **9 presentation tests pass in 2.43s**, with the new root case included in the latter (`ergonomics-editor-handoff-{edit-unit,presentation-unit}-v6.log`). The rendered fixture asserts panel bounds before capture, then exercises the normal click and keyboard presentation path. The temporary pointer/root diagnostics were removed in v7; retain their v4–v6 logs as the reproduction. V9 supplies the passing combined matrix and current feature/GUI source gates above; optimized, selected-provider and installed acceptance remain separate.

Keep the fixture on the real model/backend owners. Configure text wrap/overflow through `native::layout::configure` and `SandText.layout`; changing a temporary `TextLayout` or the legacy text setting alone is overwritten by layout resolution and does not create the intended horizontal-scroll case. The tiled fixture uses explicit `Composition::render_output` for scaled, offset or multiple outputs, preserving client-pixel assertions; the legacy combined-canvas entry is only used for its original unit-scale, zero-origin case. Physical disconnection uses `disconnect_outputs`, an empty `output_catalog` and `invalidate_outputs`, under the session feature. An empty `configure_outputs` request is correctly rejected and is not a hot-unplug simulation.

The four-scene private-provider handoff and its focused source-checkpoint regressions are complete, so their implementation/test checkbox has been removed. Keep the chosen external IME/provider, optimized and physical installed checks in their existing delivery stages. Preserve earlier ordinary-typing/protocol evidence without relabeling it as this matrix. The window-management reference's focused toolkit/capture workflows also pass; the next core gap is stage 1's creation-recovery empty editor and remaining loading/permission acceptance. ### Accepted command defaults

Confirmed requests take priority over Niri's old behavior. `Mod` means Super on this Linux setup. The following table includes the user's acceptance and latest corrections. The settings reference's source defaults, confirmed logout and the window-management reference's recent-window switcher have their implementation and focused evidence above; stage 4 retains actual input, optimized and installed acceptance. These source changes do not rewrite the live user configuration. All settings and commands use the same configuration/editor path. Additional utility commands in stages 2–3 begin unbound to avoid inventing conflicting default key families.

| Command | Default / planned chord | Decision / reason |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Workspace free ↔ tiled | Mod+T | Confirmed; whole workspace, one horizontal-row arrangement |
| Directional focus / follow | Mod+H/J/K/L | Confirmed; same row/between rows in tiling, spatial camera lock in free mode |
| Reposition item in tiling | Mod+Shift+H/J/K/L | Confirmed; reorder in row or transfer between rows |
| Focus monitor left/down/up/right | Mod+Y/U/I/O | Confirmed; replaces today's arrow combinations and displaces Mod+I inspection |
| Overview | Mod+Space | Confirmed; replaces Park's desktop Operation chord |
| Edit panel | Mod+E | Accepted compositor-wide entry; keep Alt+E in shared native canvas scope; leftmost toolbar button |
| Terminal | Mod+N | Implemented generic default; the user selects the terminal provider |
| Close focused application/item | Mod+Q | Retain; native deletion confirmation still applies where appropriate |
| Maximize in usable area | Mod+F | Implemented; preserves usable-space/bar reservations |
| Fullscreen | Mod+Shift+F | Implemented; distinct from maximize |
| Cycle focused window width | Mod+R | 100% → 50% → 33⅓% → 66⅔% → repeat; editable ordered presets |
| Recent-window switcher | Alt+Tab / Alt+Shift+Tab | MRU forward/backward; release Alt commits, Escape cancels |
| Operation | Mod+P | Confirmed after the Mod+O conflict review; shared Ctrl+K remains native-scoped |
| External application launcher | Mod+D | Retain; use the selected provider, show an actionable unset status |
| Focus / Unfocus | Mod+Backspace | Stateful toggle and bottom-right button; never interpreted as untile |
| Float / retile just the focused item | Mod+Shift+T | Confirmed; an exception within a tiled workspace |
| Select workspace 1–9 | Mod+1–9 | Retain existing behavior |
| Move focused item to workspace 1–9 | Mod+Shift+1–9 | Accepted; moves the item, not the whole row |
| Move focused item to monitor left/down/up/right | Mod+Ctrl+Y/U/I/O | Accepted Niri family, distinct from focusing a monitor |
| Move focused row to monitor left/down/up/right | Mod+Shift+Y/U/I/O | Accepted Niri-style family adapted to rows; do not alias whole-workspace movement |
| Window properties | Unbound initially | Existing application Record inspection with a clearer label; no dedicated button |
| Lock | Mod+Escape | Retain current action; selected external locker is still required |
| Saved logout | Mod+Shift+E | Implemented native confirmation; Cancel and failed saves preserve the session |

Keep whole-workspace-to-monitor, additional row-height presets and numeric resize commands discoverable in Park/Operation even when initially unbound. The accepted horizontal-width cycle remains bound to Mod+R; it is not one of those unbound additions. Row membership changes through the agreed directional move commands, without a separate exposed column/stack mode. Fullscreen is useful for games/video; maximize preserves bar-reserved space. Individual floating permits a temporary exception inside tiling. Workspace/output moves avoid reconstructing a work arrangement by hand.

**Window properties**, currently called application Record inspection, opens the existing Lince Record associated with the focused item: a Park application window or an eligible bound native item in Sandbox/Park. It allows inspection/editing of that item's Lince identity, assertions and stored properties using the existing Record inspector; Areas can use supported Record predicates. It does not inspect a web page's DOM, read an application's internal documents or automate its controls. Keep the existing advanced action available in Operation/the ordinary inspector, with no standalone toolbar button or default shortcut. A helpful example is tagging a browser window as a work item so Lince can organize it using its own Record/Area mechanisms. Unbound, unsupported or disconnected items need an actionable state rather than inventing application data or a new Record.

This inspector is approved scope. Reuse the current Record editor and mutation/validation path, keeping application-reported identity metadata distinguishable from owner-edited assertions and properties. If the live window closes while inspected, retain the Record editor when the Record remains valid and clearly indicate the missing live window. Editing properties must not implicitly launch an application or acquire additional compositor permissions. Stages 1–2 cover the remaining properties/rules integration; this is not a separate application-inspection engine.

Read-only inspection of `/home/user/.config/niri/config.kdl` informed the accepted the settings reference settings. These are general capabilities with user-local provider choices, not new mandatory dependencies:

| Niri setting / workflow | Recommendation for Park | Ownership / scope |

| Animations off | Provide an instant navigation/overview preference; target-follow always has zero relative lag regardless of animation preference | Shared interface preference, not a physics-disable switch |
| Open maximized; zero gaps; focus ring off | Offer explicit opening/layout preferences, but retain a clear selected/focused indicator in overview/editing | Do not globally maximize every native Sand, override Areas or confuse no gaps with no selection feedback |
| Brightness, audio, microphone, media hardware keys | Allow registered external argv actions using chosen brightness/audio/media tools; preserve Niri's current amounts in the user's profile | External `brightnessctl`, `wpctl`, `playerctl`; no hardware-service replacement; support XF86 key identity end to end |
| Media/brightness allowed when locked | Require an explicit per-action allow-when-locked flag, off for arbitrary commands; only chosen harmless hardware actions may opt in | Preserve lock isolation; launcher, terminal, editing and capture remain blocked |
| Print and Ctrl+Print screenshots | Bind external screenshot/selection actions if the chosen provider supports them | Existing capture authorization/portal path, no second screenshot UI stack |

| Editor launch on Mod+Z | Optional user external-action example | Do not install or hardcode the user's editor |

Do not import Niri's startup list into Park: UWSM and selected providers still own service startup. Its Noctalia start/kill shortcuts, personal PDF shell pipelines, clipboard download command, synthesized `@` workaround and debug activation bypass are user-specific choices, not default Park features. Keep external commands as literal argv descriptions and represent any intentional shell script explicitly as the user's chosen program. Do not weaken activation checks to copy Niri's debug option. A shortcut editor manages registered external actions as well as built-in commands, but does not execute actions while capturing keys.

### Accepted family coverage and deferred scope

The previously reviewed useful desktop families are now requirements, not an unselected comparison catalogue. Acceptance covers the families listed in the table below; it does not adopt every feature, default or implementation from the referenced desktops. This mapping makes their implementation and evidence traceable. The sources are interaction references; they do not change Park's Smithay/Bevy foundation or introduce dependencies on another compositor. They follow the selected core workflow rather than expanding suggestion 5 into advanced pointer behavior. Retain three separate completion states for each family: implemented behavior and controls, automated integration acceptance, and any still-required provider or physical-device acceptance. An unavailable hardware capability must not erase its outstanding acceptance or cause a replacement service to be written.

Implement the selected core suggestions in the window-management reference before the additional families. Stage 2's overview dragging builds on the navigation reference's controller and the window-management reference's interaction ownership; its opening rules build on the window-management reference's placement/activation path. Stage 3's display capabilities extend the display reference's output transaction, and its capture/effect work reuses the rendering reference's rendering budgets. Complete each family's backend binding, existing UI destination and relevant checks together. Where another program owns a service, the task is capability negotiation, the general adapter if missing and integration testing with the user's selected provider. Adding a family does not authorize building its external service from scratch.

Within stage 2, finish Window properties before its opening-rule controls, preserving the stable inspected Record identity across focus changes. Within stage 3, use the existing output/capture/input capability boundaries before adding controls for dependent effects or hardware. Follow the listed family order where there is no dependency; record a capability-dependent acceptance gap against that family while continuing independent feature work. Keep the general adapters reusable and the user's selected programs as the initial fixtures. For each family, retain its dependency, shared-versus-Park owner, ordinary UI destination and acceptance together; adding a command without its usable interaction is unfinished. These follow-up requirements remain separate from the simple core pointer milestone.

| Accepted family | Implementation owner and ordinary destination | Required acceptance |
| --- | --- | --- |

| Cyclic horizontal sizes and manual resize | the settings and window-management references; one Mod+R action, the Window layout modal and shared manual size commands | Exact full/half/third/two-thirds intent, repeat suppression, saved custom order/chord and real-client constraints; remaining integrated input/installed checks in stage 4 |

Use the existing destinations in this table without adding another permanent desktop toolbar or a second layout picker. The split entries for compact groups, scratch windows and windowed fullscreen keep their completion gates independent; they are the already accepted utility family. Provider-dependent controls explain the unavailable capability and required external choice. These destinations do not add default key families or approve the deferred other-desktop ideas.

Preserve the two execution modes when assigning each family's owner:

| Boundary | Required behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Shared interface / Sandbox and Park | Rows, width presets, eligible native-item focus/MRU, workspace navigation, overview, Record editing and interface configuration use the same model/actions and native UI. Sandbox gestures apply inside its own canvas and cannot intercept unrelated host applications. |
| Park compositor | Global application-window management, monitor/device settings, host-level focus/input routing, scratch/group visibility and output composition use Smithay plus the existing host bridge. Sandbox exposes only capabilities supplied by its current host; it does not claim control of that host's windows or monitors. |
| External provider / hardware | Screenshot/sharing, accessibility reader, hardware controls and session/power services use the chosen general provider/protocol. Test available capabilities and keep missing integration or physical evidence explicit; no service is reimplemented to fill an unavailable provider. |

For shared features, verify both a native Sandbox item and a real Park Wayland client where applicable. For compositor-only features, verify that Sandbox keeps working and presents capability availability accurately. These checks extend stage 4 rather than creating another implementation stage or a separate copy of each interaction.

For each family, first identify the existing Lince action/model, Smithay facility or external protocol/provider that owns it. Implement only the missing shared behavior and its binding/UI, then verify the complete interaction. A registered command or visible settings control alone is not completion; neither is an unavailable provider a reason to implement that provider inside Park. Keep outstanding provider/hardware acceptance in the corresponding checklist while removing only the feature work and tests that have actually passed.
